# Review log: Sidecar runtime processes have no platform supervisor

## Standing context

### Settled

- **Re-baseline of 2026-10-22.** The draft of 2026-10-01 was written while proposal 0079 was a draft and before the owner decisions of 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-02 (plan §10.3), the security review of 2026-10-02, and proposals 0090 to 0094. It was re-baselined against the tree at `f52fc9a49` and the plan's Phase 2 entry for 0087 part 1. What changed, and why:
  - Part 2 (cross-tenant reuse, the tenant-pin reset, `allowCrossTenantReuse`) moved to proposal 0096, as the plan's entry requires.
  - Scope widened from opt-in supervision selected by an optional `command` to every sidecar pod of a `type: agent` runtime, with `command` required on the Runtime CRD (plan §10.3, supervisor scope). The admin registration payload does not carry `command`; D-COMMAND keeps `command` and `env` CRD-only, and D-SCOPE leaves `type: mcp` runtimes unsupervised. The owner approved both on 2026-10-22 (`[owner-decisions.2026-10-22]`).
  - The lifetime selector moved to the pool field `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep | restart`, required with no default, with `restart` refused through a mirrored `restartable` flag and exempt from `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` on sequential pools (plan §10.3, 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-02). The draft's D-SELECT, D-ACK, and the non-goals that rejected a pool field were removed.
  - The supervisor became a static C binary in its own digest-pinned image with a handler for every catchable signal (plan §10.3, gVisor spike; security review findings 1 and 2). Its signal model is now the **Signals.** table ("Signal model home" below). The draft's Go `supervise` subcommand of the adapter (D-BINARY) was withdrawn.
  - The supervisor is a boundary for the reuse decision rather than "never an isolation control", and it ends the runtime before `cleanupCommands` rather than inside step 1 (security review finding 2; plan §10.3).
  - `preConnect` and service-mode runtimes are supervised rather than refused (D-SDKWARM, D-SERVICE; F-6.1.28).
  - `CH-SUPERVISE` gained a generation and request ID on every message, enumerated error codes, strict decoding, a liveness ping, mutual peer-UID authentication, the `sweep` request, and the unsolicited `exited` report (findings 3, 6, 8, 10, and 12; F-4.7.27).
  - D-STALE was rewritten: since proposal 0090 the runtime writes first on `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`, so the draft's argument that the adapter's first write rejects a stale peer is false. The adapter now closes and rebinds its runtime listeners between an attested end and the next launch (finding 4 for the adapter's own listeners; finding 11).
  - The draft's SPEC-6 (agent UID only on the runtime container) is already true: §13.1 **Container identity.** (proposal 0088) reserves the agent and adapter UIDs. The same rule forbids the draft's stage init container at the adapter UID, so the stage container runs at a dedicated UID, and SPEC-6 now states the supervisor volume's mount rule.
  - Every staged spec edit now quotes current tree text. The draft quoted 0079's staging.
  - New homes for F-4.7.26, F-4.7.27, F-5.2.36, F-5.2.37 (cleanup half), F-5.2.39 (step 3), F-5.2.46, and F-6.1.28, and for 0091's request to state the crash outcome through the §7.3 resume flow.

- **SDK-warm lifecycle home (redesign of 2026-10-22).** The sidecar SDK-warm lifecycle has one home, SPEC-2 §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **SDK-warm.** **Launch.** is the one home of the warm-up READY gate. §4.7.10 lifetime, **End.**, SPEC-3(a), SPEC-8(d), SPEC-9(a), the §6.1 compatibility row (SPEC-9(c)), the §6.2 scrub-warning paragraph (SPEC-9(d)), D-SDKWARM, and CODE-3 cite it. Do not restate the lifecycle at a citing site.
- **Runtime sweep home (redesign of 2026-10-22).** What the sweep removes has one home, SPEC-2 §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Sweep.**, whose volume list is the runtime container's writable mounts in `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go`. SPEC-5(c), (d), and (e), D-SWEEP, and CODE-2 cite it. Do not restate the list.

- **Runtime-process lifetime terms (second redesign of 2026-10-22).** SPEC-5(b) §5.2 **Runtime-process lifetime (`recycle.runtimeProcess`).** defines "a `keep` pod" and "a `restart` pod" as pods whose lifetime, fixed at creation from the pool's `recycle.runtimeProcess` (or `keep` on a pool without the field) and carried by the label `lenny.dev/runtime-process`, is `keep` or `restart`. Every staged statement that depends on the lifetime uses those terms. `recycle.runtimeProcess` is named only at pod creation, at pool admission, and in the acknowledgment requirement. Do not write "under `keep`", "under `restart`", "a pool that keeps its runtime process", or "a `restart` pool" in a lifetime-dependent sentence.
- **Owner decisions of 2026-10-22.** The owner approved D-SCOPE (only `type: agent` sidecar runtimes are supervised; `type: mcp` runtimes stay unsupervised and without `command` until the proposal that defines their launch, recorded as the `type: mcp` supervision follow-up in gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2), D-COMMAND (`command` and `env` are settable only on the Runtime CRD), and D-RESTARTGATE (pool admission refuses `recycle.runtimeProcess: restart` until proposal 0084 lands). D-RESTARTGATE is a code-only gate in CODE-5 `validateRecyclePolicy` that returns `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, with comments citing the intra-pod MCP nonce rule and stating that 0084 removes it. Do not stage the interim refusal as specification text, and do not reopen any of the three.
- **Scrub steps 1 and 1b on supervised pods (2026-10-22).** Steps 1 and 1b run in the adapter container on every pod, supervised or not, because setup commands run in the adapter process at the adapter UID (`Server.RunSetup` in `pkg/adapter/staging.go` calls `workspace.RunSetup`, whose cancel kills only the command's process group, so a `setsid` descendant survives). SPEC-5(d) edits step 3 only, and SPEC-5(e) states that steps 1 and 1b run on every pod. Do not reintroduce a supervised-pod exemption.
- **Property self-test carrier (second redesign of 2026-10-22).** The supervisor always dials and sends `ready` first, carrying `properties` (`pass` or `fail` per enumerated property). The adapter logs each failed property and, on a `restart` pod, exits non-zero before READY when the non-dumpable or kill-reach property failed (a cause before READY under SPEC-2 **Liveness.**). SPEC-4 item 8 **Property self-test.** is the home; the §28.5.3 `CH-SUPERVISE` card states the message. `--require-properties` is withdrawn.

- **Supervised scrub sequence home (third redesign of 2026-10-22).** The outcome of each step of the whole-pod scrub on a pod that runs the runtime supervisor, and of the launch that follows it on a `restart` pod, has one home: the SPEC-5(c) §5.2 **Supervised scrub sequence.** table, including which rows an earlier row's outcome skips (`[spec.22.fix-G1.1]`). SPEC-2 **End.**, **Sweep.**, **Cleanup.**, and **Crash.**, the SPEC-7 card's **Preconditions.** and **Degradation.**, the §28.8 row, the edge-case table, D-CLEANUP, D-SWEEP, D-CRASH, CODE-2, CODE-3, and TEST-1 cite it. Do not restate a step's outcome at a citing site.
- **Signal model home (third redesign of 2026-10-22).** The supervisor's action for each signal source has one home: the SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Signals.** bullet and its table. No action depends on a signal's sender, so the `si_pid`/`si_code` stop-request filter is withdrawn and sender identification is retired (owner decisions of 2026-10-22 below). The **Pod termination.** bullet is merged into the table. D-SIGNALS, D-PODEXIT, D-PROPERTIES, the edge-case table, CODE-2, and TEST-1 cite it. Do not restate a signal action at a citing site, and do not reintroduce a sender filter without an action that needs it.
- **Owner decisions of 2026-10-22 on the supervisor's security review (fourth redesign).** Do not reopen any of these.
  - Sender identification is retired (owner decision, after a security review found the design equivalent or better without it). It is gone from the self-test, the `restart` gate, `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor`, SPEC-4 item 8, CODE-2, CODE-3, CODE-6, and TEST-1, because no supervisor action reads a signal's sender. The gating properties are non-dumpable inspection and kill reach; PID-namespace init signal immunity is reported only (D-PROPERTIES). Do not reintroduce `SA_SIGINFO`, an `si_pid` read, or a sender property without an action that needs it.
  - Fault signals (decided under the owner's delegation; replaces delegated decision (A) of `[spec.22.fix-G2.1]`): no handler for SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGFPE, or SIGILL; they stay `SIG_DFL` and blocked in the supervisor, and each child unblocks every signal before `execve`. A synchronous fault is forced through and ends the supervisor; a sent one never takes effect. Home: the SPEC-2 **Signals.** table (D-SIGNALS). Do not restore an exit-on-fault handler.
  - Implementation hazards (decided under the owner's delegation) are CODE-2 requirements with TEST-1 cases: SIGCHLD flag cleared before the reap loop, close-on-exec on every supervisor and worker descriptor, `EINTR` retry on every syscall, a flag-only handler or a block for every signal 1 to `_NSIG - 1`, no SIGALRM timer, and a kill-reach self-test that sees the grandchild in `/proc` before the kill (D-HAZARDS).
  - Accepted bound (decided under the owner's delegation): a process a cleanup command moves out of its process group, with `setsid` or otherwise, escapes the deadline SIGKILL; a `restart` pod's second end ends it, and a `keep` pod keeps it as it keeps the runtime. Stated once, in SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** Do not file it as a finding.
- **Child-process model home (fourth redesign of 2026-10-22).** What the supervisor reports when each kind of child exits (the launched runtime process, a sweep worker, the cleanup worker, each cleanup command, and processes reparented to the namespace init), and how a status reaches the step that waits for it, has one home: the SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Children.** bullet and its table. One reaper collects every child; a status that does not reach its waiting step is a failure. The scrub sequence table's `cleanupCommands` Error cell, the Signals table's SIGCHLD row, the edge-case table, D-CHILDREN, D-CLEANUP, CODE-2 (`end`, `sweep`, `cleanup`, `exited`, reaping), and TEST-1 cite it. This supersedes `[spec.23.fix-G1.1]`'s choice of the `cleanupCommands` Error cell as the home of the cleanup failure conditions. Do not list child exit conditions at a citing site.
- **Not-live latch home (fourth redesign of 2026-10-22; extended by the fifth).** The adapter's not-live state has one home: the SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Liveness.** bullet and its table, which state every cause (as labeled causes; *hang* and a `CH-MSGSOCK` end while a session is held are causes on every pod since the fifth redesign), that the state refuses every session start (one that would use a live process included) and every `launch`, that nothing clears it, and that `ReportPodScrub` reports it so the pod retires; before READY a cause exits the adapter, and on a service-mode pod any cause does. **Launch.**, **SDK-warm.**, SPEC-4 item 8, the scrub sequence table's definitions and launch row, the edge-case table, D-NOTLIVE, and CODE-3's state table cite it. Do not restate a cause or the refusal at a citing site.
- **Not-live reclaim table home (fifth redesign of 2026-10-22).** How a pod on which the adapter's not-live state is set, or whose adapter exited for a cause, retires has one home: the table under the SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Liveness.** bullet. Rows are the `Sandbox` phases `warming`, `sdk_connecting` on the warm-fill edge, `idle`, `reserved`, `claimed` with a `bound` claim on a sequential and on a concurrent pod, `claimed` while the whole-pod scrub runs (claim `recycling`), `sdk_connecting` on the recycle re-warm edge, and termination; columns are service-mode, session `keep`, `restart`, and `preConnect` pods; each cell names the causes (the labeled causes of **Liveness.**) and one channel: **Exit**, **Start**, **Claim**, **Report**, or **None**. The table lives in §4.7.10 rather than §6.2 because its cells are the adapter's causes, which **Liveness.** owns; §6.2 owns only the edges, which the table cites and SPEC-2(f) extends with the adapter's exit. The §5.2 **Runtime not live:** item (SPEC-5(i)), the SPEC-7 card's **Degradation.**, the §28.8 row, the edge-case table, D-HUNG, D-NOTLIVE, D-SERVICE, CODE-3, and TEST-1 cite it. Do not state a reclaim path at a citing site, and do not add a §6.2 paragraph for the adapter's exit.
- **Precursor WarmPoolController fix (owner decision of 2026-10-22).** The defect in which `observePod` keys only on Pod phase (a terminated `adapter` container on a `Running` pod is invisible) and the `Sandbox` phase `failed` has no reclaimer is fixed by a separate direct fix on `fix/warmpool-reclaim-terminated-adapter`, which lands before 0087. 0087 cites it as a precondition (summary D-PREREQS and **Watch out for.**, the checklist preamble) and stages no change to `observePod`, `Decide`, or `DecideSDKWarm`. The **Exit** channel assumes that fix excludes a pod that is being deleted. Do not re-stage the controller change, and do not file the missing reclaimer against 0087.
- **Cleanup bound names (prune of 2026-10-22).** Two values bound the supervised `cleanupCommands`, and each has one name and one home. The `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` deadline, at which the cleanup worker sends SIGKILL to the running command's process group, is defined in SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** The `cleanup` request bound, after which an unanswered `cleanup` is a link failure and which exceeds the `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` deadline, is defined in the SPEC-7 `CH-SUPERVISE` card's **Timing.** bullet. The Signals table, the scrub sequence table, the edge-case table, D-CLEANUP, CODE-2, and CODE-3 use those names. Do not write "the deadline of the `cleanup` request" or "the request bound" for either value.

### Traps

- The plan's Phase 2 entry was written on 2026-10-12. Several of its statements are stale (the Ledger block below lists them). Read the tree, not the entry, for current facts.
- `scrub.DefaultOps` steps 1 and 1b exec `sh` and `ipcrm`, which the distroless adapter image does not contain.
- An admin-registered runtime has no pod path today unless a Runtime CRD of the same name exists. The pod builder reads `command` from the CRD, and the RuntimeReconciler mirrors only `restartable` into the registry, where admission reads it. Do not stage `command` or `env` on the admin payload; the owner approved D-COMMAND on 2026-10-22.

### Open

- The owner decided the three open decisions on 2026-10-22 (`[owner-decisions.2026-10-22]`) and retired the sender-identification property the same day (`[redesign4.2026-10-22]`). No owner question is open.

### Deferred

- Relaunch after a crash, and pre-launching a session-mode runtime before its session arrives (plan §10.2, **Not yet scheduled**).
- Supervision of `type: mcp` runtimes, in the proposal that defines their launch (the `type: mcp` supervision follow-up in gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2).
- Admission of `recycle.runtimeProcess: restart`, which waits for proposal 0084 (D-RESTARTGATE).

## Ledger

### [f1.cleanup]

FACT: Open-decisions firing 1 cleanup found the summary already carrying the listed sections in order, with an empty open-decisions section, and moved no content.

### [rebaseline.2026-10-22]

DECISION: Part 2 moved to proposal 0096 (`proposals/0096_new_cross-tenant-reuse-on-in-place-pools-by-resetting-the-tenant-pin/`); 0087 keeps a one-line pointer (D-PART2).
DECISION: `recycle.runtimeProcess` is required on every pool 0079's keep rule covers, concurrent pools included, and rejected elsewhere. The owner's decision names new sequential recycling pools; extending it to concurrent pools keeps one rule and an explicit choice everywhere a pod is reused.
DECISION: The property self-test gates on the non-dumpable mark, sender identification, and `kill(-1)` reach. PID-namespace init signal immunity is reported and does not gate, because the owner offered `restart` on gVisor, where it fails, on the ground that its failure fails closed. Plan §10.3 says "the four properties"; this reading reconciles the two owner decisions.
DECISION: The property check is a per-pod startup self-test, fail-closed on `restart` pods, plus `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor` for a named RuntimeClass, following the §4.7.11 `SO_PEERCRED` self-test precedent rather than a stored per-RuntimeClass result.
DECISION: The supervisor is freestanding C with no libc, which satisfies both readings of finding 1 ("no libc or NSS loading" in the plan, "no libc or musl" in the review).
DECISION: Image `ENTRYPOINT`, `CMD`, and `ENV` are unused on sidecar pods; the runtime gets the platform's variables and a fixed `PATH` (D-ENV). No `Runtime.spec.env` field is added. Superseded by D-RUNTIMEENV (`[delegated.2026-10-22]`).
DECISION: `restart` stays refused on `preConnect` and service-mode runtimes, as the owner's admission-predicate decision states, while both are supervised under `keep`. The plan's flag name "can be supervised" no longer fits, because every sidecar runtime is supervised; the flag is named `restartable`.
DECISION: A runtime crash fails each bound session with `runtime_crash` into proposal 0091's funnel and retires the pod at its next boundary on every pool (D-CRASH), which answers 0091's impacts row.
DECISION: F-5.2.46 is closed by marking the runtime not live on a heartbeat escalation on a `keep` pod (D-HUNG). The supervisor ping detects a dead or frozen supervisor, not a hung runtime, so it cannot close F-5.2.46 as BUILD-GAPS and 0091 state.
DECISION: 0087 does not render `--runtime-ops-socket`. R17 renders it and inherits the per-generation rebind (D-STALE).
DECISION: At pod termination the supervisor forwards the kubelet's SIGTERM at once (D-PODEXIT). The adapter shutdown and eviction proposal may replace the forward with an adapter request.
DECISION: The lifetime reaches the adapter on the boundary's `Shutdown` recycle disposition, so a pool edit applies at each pod's next boundary. Flagged for the S-2 proto window. Superseded by D-PODLIFETIME (`[delegated.2026-10-22]`).
FACT: Proposals 0090, 0091, 0092, 0093, and 0094 and the credential fail-open fix (F-4.9.36, merge `d05c25630`) have landed. P1 is 0093 and P5 is 0094, both implemented, so 0087 part 1's prerequisites in plan §10.2 are met except its own approval.
FACT: F-4.7.25 is closed by proposal 0090; `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` authenticate a runtime-first nonce line against the `mcpNonce` published at accept time, which rotates at each session start.
FACT: §13.1 **Container identity.** reserves the agent UID for the regular container `runtime` and the adapter UID for `adapter` on every container kind, and the test-only egress-capture container runs at UID 65531.
FACT: The preStop hook execs `/usr/local/bin/lenny prestop` (F-4.6.37, closed); F-4.6.38 stays open and belongs to R16.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.2 Phase 0 still says the `SO_PEERCRED` half of F-4.7.25 and the hardening branch items are on branch `worktree-agent-a14863c1e63bb8ccf`, and that F-4.7.25 "stays open for the manifest nonce"; F-4.7.25 is closed.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.2 Phase 2's credential fail-open entry says the fix is "not yet merged"; it is merged (`d05c25630`).
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.2 Phase 2's 0087 entry omits F-5.2.46, which BUILD-GAPS and 0091 assign to 0087 part 1, and it attributes that finding's fix to the supervisor liveness ping, which cannot detect a hung runtime.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.3's admission-predicate decision names the mirrored flag "can be supervised" and makes it false for `preConnect` and service-mode runtimes, while the supervisor-scope decision of the same day supervises both. The flag decides `restart`, not supervision.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.3 says "the four properties" gate `restart` on microVM pools and in the conformance check, while the gVisor decision offers `restart` where the signal-immunity property fails.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.3 states "admin-registered runtimes are all sidecars"; the tree has no pod path for an admin-only runtime, and the specification does not state the deployment model of one.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.6 says 0087 "supervises only restart pools" and that under `restart` "the single-session SDKs stay correct with no change"; supervision is universal, and since 0090 the SDKs are multi-session.
FACT: Stale plan statement: R17's re-derivation calls D-STALE "0087's stale-binary decision"; D-STALE concerns stale connections, and the draft's version of it was false after 0090.
FACT: Stale plan statement: §10.2's Phase 2 entry says the hardening "states the pod-wide agent-UID rule that 0087's mutual authentication on `CH-SUPERVISE` relies on" and lists "a pod-level runAsUser default that is neither the agent nor the adapter UID"; the tree sets no pod-level identity (`base_pod.go`), and §13.1 states that a pod-level value does not satisfy the rule.

### [delegated.2026-10-22]

DECISION: `cleanupCommands` run at the agent UID in the runtime container through the supervisor's forked worker on every sidecar pod, under both lifetimes, with a second `end` after them on `restart` pools (D-CLEANUP). Decided under the owner's delegation, closing open decision 1 with option (b). It closes the F-5.2.36 confused deputy on `keep` pools and the gap that the commands ran in the shell-less distroless adapter image. `CH-SUPERVISE` gains `cleanup` and `cleaned`.
DECISION: `Runtime.spec.env`, an optional list of `name` and `value` pairs with the `LENNY_` prefix reserved and refused at admission, mirrored through the registry and the admin payload as `command` is, and set by the supervisor after `clearenv()` (D-RUNTIMEENV). Decided under the owner's delegation, replacing "no `Runtime.spec.env` field".
DECISION: The pod's lifetime is fixed at pod creation and reaches the adapter by the `--runtime-process` flag, with a pod label the acquisition reads (D-PODLIFETIME). No proto field is added. The disposition field it replaces needed a proto field, and it left two holes the label closes: a pod `reserved` or `idle` after a `keep` boundary served one more session in its kept process after an edit to `restart` that removed the acknowledgment, and a pod created without `--require-properties` ran `restart` without the property gate. Decided under the owner's delegation, which preferred the no-proto alternative where it is as simple.
FACT: Plan §10.3's admission-predicate decision says `command` stays CRD-only, and the later supervisor-scope decision adds `command` to the admin registration API. The proposal follows the supervisor-scope decision (D-COMMAND), and `env` follows `command`.
FACT: The step-0 cleanup metadata is not wired in the tree (`podscrub.go` `scrubConfig` sets neither `PrevProvider` nor `PrevLeaseID`). Recorded under the summary's defects not staged.
FACT: Re-review of 2026-10-22 corrected these staged anchors against the tree at `f52fc9a49`: the hard-wrapped find strings of SPEC-2(a), SPEC-5(b), and SPEC-7(a); the non-unique `maxSessionsPerPod: 50` anchor of SPEC-5(a); the `#### Normative Merge Algorithm` heading and the first `#### Derived Runtime` heading of SPEC-1; the CH-MSGSOCK **Timing.** bullet of SPEC-8(a), whose wording differs from **Inbound: `shutdown`**; the third `RUNTIME_CRASH` site of SPEC-8(c); and the Generation bullet's citation, whose rule lives in §28.6 rather than §15.4.3. It added SPEC-2(d) for the §4.7.1 `Shutdown` row, the `keep` scoping of the occupancy-zero sentence of **Runtime process lifetime.**, and the SPEC-5(g) edits to the acknowledgment paragraph's first sentence and its edit rule, and it completed the list of `KeepsRuntime` readers.

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Launch.** is the one home of the launch points and the launch permit (no process launched since pod creation or the last attested end); SPEC-3(b) step 7 and SPEC-9 point to it — BECAUSE the predicate was written at three sites and the "after an end" gate blocked the DemoteSDK relaunch — ALTERNATIVES: keeping "when no process is live" (relaunches an idle-exited keep process; G2's finding); a DemoteSDK exception on the old gate (wrong scope).
DECISION: SPEC-5(c)'s order sentence is the only statement of the restart-boundary order; SPEC-2 **End.** and SPEC-7(d) **Preconditions.** cite §5.2 **Lenny scrub procedure.** — BECAUSE the copies disagreed on `sweep` at DemoteSDK — ALTERNATIVES: syncing three copies.
WATCHOUT: `DemoteSDK` is `end` only, with no `sweep`. SPEC-7 once sent `sweep` on DemoteSDK; a sweep at claim time deletes agent-owned files during a claim and adds nothing on `keep`. Do not reintroduce it. — EVIDENCE: summary D-SDKWARM; non-spec CODE-3 SDK-warm bullet.
WATCHOUT: The `scrub_warning` launch block was removed from the Launch bullet. Under `onScrubFailure: warn` (default) a pod serves the next session (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md, `warn` bullet), so the block made every warn-policy `restart` pod fail its next start. A failed end or sweep already retires the pod via Liveness. Because of this, CODE-3's `EndRuntimeAgain` must run even when cleanup failed.
WATCHOUT: The SPEC-9 re-warm-edge edit to the `preConnect` compatibility table was deleted. The re-warm edge launches nothing, because a demoted session already launched at step 7. Do not restore "which launches a new runtime process when the session demoted the earlier one".
WATCHOUT: The rebind after an attested end covers only `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`. "Every runtime listener" contradicted D-STALE and the MCP once-per-pod sites. The MCP surface is already re-armed with the new nonce on every sole start (`claimPodMCPStartLocked` in pkg/adapter/slotsession.go; `startPlatformMCP(nonce)` in pkg/adapter/session.go), so a relaunched process authenticates. The spec's "started at most once per pod" (spec/04_system-components.md, mcpNonce row) disagrees with that code, but the mismatch predates 0087 and belongs to 0084. Do not stage a nonce re-arm here.
FACT: The §4.7.10 preConnect sentence's find string `the agent process and which `DemoteSDK` tears down and the SDK re-warm restarts, is` is unique in spec/04_system-components.md. The §28.5.3 reason clause `, because `DemoteSDK` does not end the runtime process ([§4.7.10](04_system-components.md#4710-deployment-model))` sits on spec/28_communication-channels.md:692 — EVIDENCE: grep at f52fc9a49-based worktree.
FACT: "before spawning the runtime" appears at spec/28_communication-channels.md:814 and :868; "before the runtime binary is spawned" appears at spec/04_system-components.md:796. SPEC-11(a) and (b) now cover them. docs/reference/adapter-contract.md also has "before spawning" wording, which belongs to DOC-1.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0087/proposals/0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7/0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: S6 says it "Lands `CH-SUPERVISE` in §28 and the `shutdown` and exit-code corrections". SPEC-7 now also has (g), which deletes the DemoteSDK reason clause from the §28.5.3 session-frame write-order row. S6 should also name that row edit.
OPEN: CODE-3 still says "On a `keep` pod it composes one `SocketRuntimeProcess` for the pod's life". A demoted relaunch on a `keep` `preConnect` pod needs a new composed transport and generation (D-TRANSPORT, D-COHORT). The non-spec loop should own this.
OPEN: SPEC-3(a) gates READY on a `preConnect` runtime until the supervisor has launched the process. When the SDK-warm circuit breaker disables warm-up, nothing launches at warm-up. The Launch permit then lets step 7 launch, but the READY gate as written would hold the pod un-READY. This is a pre-existing gap that a later round should check.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** is the single list of not-live conditions; it is lifetime-scoped (an exit other than by an `end` counts on a keep pod at any time, on a restart pod only while a session is bound), a failed launch/end/sweep counts on every pod, and a hung escalation counts only on keep — BECAUSE three copies (Liveness, SPEC-5(c), SPEC-7 Degradation) disagreed and the fail-closed answer is every pod — ALTERNATIVES: restart-only end/sweep scope (fails open on a keep pod whose DemoteSDK end failed).
DECISION: **Crash.** excludes an exit caused by an `end`, and the card says `exited` is sent only for such exits — BECAUSE DemoteSDK and the `shutdown` deadline `end` run with a session bound — ALTERNATIVES: adapter ignores `exited` while an `end` is outstanding (timing rule; supervisor already knows which exits it caused).
DECISION: a service-mode pod reaching any Liveness condition makes its adapter exit non-zero — BECAUSE agent pods use RestartPolicy Never and lifecycle.Decide drains an Idle Sandbox whose pod Failed, while a Running NotReady pod is left alone — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37; pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:87-97.
WATCHOUT: writing "the pod is not ready" for a service-mode exit (old D-SERVICE) builds nothing; no controller replaces a Running NotReady idle pod — EVIDENCE: lifecycle.go:94.
WATCHOUT: the strict-decode "matches nothing outstanding" test cannot apply to the unsolicited `exited`; Degradation now scopes it to replies, and an `exited` fails only on a non-current `gen` — EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-7(d) **Degradation.**
FACT: §5.2 "Runtime not live" already says a runtime that stops after the report is found at the next start and the start fails — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:527.
UNVERIFIED: service-mode Sandboxes sit in phase idle so lifecycle.Decide's Idle+PodFailed arm fires; the tier-5 service-mode replacement test (not staged here) should confirm.
OPEN: edge row "The supervisor misses a `pong` deadline ..." and D-PING say bound sessions fail on a link failure, but no staged spec text (Crash or Liveness) states that bound sessions fail on a link failure. A later round should give it a home.
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.non-spec-changes.md]: TEST-1 Tier 5 has no service-mode replacement case (kill the service runtime; the pod reaches Failed and a replacement becomes Ready); the non-spec loop authors it. Not added here because this lane may not author.

### [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: CH-SUPERVISE authority direction is `Both` (register row and card Axes) — BECAUSE §28.2 defines it as the originating participant and `exited` is unsolicited from the supervisor; CH-MSGSOCK and CH-RUNTIMEOPS set the precedent — ALTERNATIVES: `Pod adapter` with `exited` read as a reply, rejected because no request is outstanding when it is sent. This closes the standing open item "§28.3 authority direction for `exited`". EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:82, :124-125
DECISION: the CH-SUPERVISE card's **Exclusivity.** bullet is the one home of the guards (listener close after the first pre-READY accept; supervisor exit on close). §28.6 and the §28.8 exclusivity cell are pointers — BECAUSE §28.6's lead says it states no constraint the cards do not carry (spec/28_communication-channels.md:1804) — ALTERNATIVES: §28.6 as home, rejected because it inverts that contract.
DECISION: the supervisor repeats a refused dial (ECONNREFUSED only) until connected, with no supervisor-side deadline; the adapter's bounded wait before READY is the fail-closed bound — BECAUSE adapter and runtime are both regular containers under RestartPolicy Never (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37) with no bind ordering, so a single dial fails random startups — ALTERNATIVES: postStart or native-sidecar ordering (new pod mechanism; a startup-probe gate deadlocks on READY), reversed dial direction (moves the race), supervisor retry deadline (timing heuristic the owner rejects).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "the supervisor never dials a second time / never redials" anywhere; it contradicts the Timing retry. Exclusivity after connect rests on listener close plus exit-on-close. An accepted connection that fails the adapter-UID check is not retried; the supervisor exits.
WATCHOUT: do not write "retries within the adapter's wait" — the supervisor cannot observe that wait, and the wording implies a supervisor deadline.
DECISION: SPEC-4 **Why each end trusts the other.** now points to the §28.5.3 card for the peer-UID checks (the card conditions both checks on SO_PEERCRED enforcement; SPEC-4 had conditioned only the adapter's). SPEC-4 keeps the ordering reason and the nonce-only sentence the edge-case row "Nonce-only mode" cites.
DECISION: SPEC-6 supervisor-volume rule excludes by name: "no container other than the init container `lenny-supervisor-stage` mounts it read-write" — BECAUSE the earlier "regular or ephemeral" wording exempted native sidecars (init containers with restartPolicy: Always) and later init containers — ALTERNATIVES: naming the volume in spec (implementation constant; §13.1's credential rule does not name its volume), listing init-container kinds (goes stale).
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: no deliverable was added, removed, or resequenced by G3; nothing owed.

DECISION: SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** is the one home of how the supervisor runs cleanupCommands (agent UID, forked worker, workspace base, environment, aggregate bound); SPEC-5(c)/(e) cite **Runtime supervisor.**; home table rows updated to match — BECAUSE worker, UID, environment, and deadline are supervisor behaviour already homed in SPEC-2 — ALTERNATIVES: homing it in SPEC-5 (splits the supervisor's description across §4.7.10 and §5.2).
DECISION: the no-CH-SUPERVISE-descriptor rule for workers stays only in SPEC-4 **Protection from author code.** (it also covers the sweep worker); the Cleanup bullet no longer states it — BECAUSE it is a security predicate whose argument lives in SPEC-4 item 8 — ALTERNATIVES: keeping it in Cleanup and cutting SPEC-4 (loses the property where the trust argument rests on it).
FACT: cleanupTimeoutSeconds is an aggregate bound on the whole cleanupCommands phase — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go CleanupTimeout doc comment ("bounds the whole cleanupCommands phase"); SPEC-7(d) `cleanup` carries one timeoutMs; non-spec CODE `cleanup` worker kills at the single `timeoutMs` deadline.
MISTAKE: an earlier revision stated the bound per command in SPEC-2 Cleanup and the edge row, which would let N slow commands outrun the gateway's missing-report timeout (cleanupTimeoutSeconds plus grace) and retire the pod instead of failing the scrub under onScrubFailure.
WATCHOUT: the staged edit to §5.2 **Recycle lifecycle** (cleanupCommands "at the agent UID in the runtime container") was deleted as a restatement; the tree sentence names no executor and stays true. Do not re-add it — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md **Recycle lifecycle (`recycle.enabled: true`).**
OPEN: SPEC-2 has no **Sweep.** bullet and the home table assigns the sweep mechanism nowhere, although SPEC-5(c) points to **Runtime supervisor.** for it. Pre-existing; a later round should file it.

DECISION: SPEC-5(b) names the predicate "reusing recycling pool" and is its one home; SPEC-5(a) comment, SPEC-5(g) acknowledgment and reuse sentences, and the §5.2 "recycling configurations outside the rule" sentence cite the name — BECAUSE (g) added `keep` as a fourth conjunct and the YAML comment's "where the acknowledgment rule applies" became circular — ALTERNATIVES: defining the term in the acknowledgment paragraph (later in §5.2; it owns the acknowledgment, not where the field is required).
DECISION: Seven hold/drain sites (§4.6.1 table row and Reserved hold, §4.6.3 ownership row, §5.2 Pinned idle inventory, §6.2 diagram edge and `reserved` hold semantics, §29.2 step) cite the acknowledgment's per-pod refusal in SPEC-5(h) — BECAUSE a pool predicate catches every sequential `restart` pool — ALTERNATIVES: "on a pool that does not reuse pods" misses the per-pod refusal of used keep-created pods after an edit.

### [spec.1.fix-G6.1]
DECISION: `command` and `env` are CRD-only, following the `requireSoPeercred` precedent in §5.1; SPEC-10 deleted with its home-table row, deliverable-index line, and the admin halves of CODE-1 (RuntimePayload bullet, admin/runtimes.go and openapi Runtime targets, Tier-1 admin case) — BECAUSE §15.1 has no payload field description to anchor to (only the endpoint rows at spec/15_external-api-surface.md:803/806 and the dry-run row at :1223), the pod builder reads the live CRD, and `lenny up` seeds the embedded `echo` (pkg/embedded/stack/runtimes.go, "deploymentModel: embedded") and the §26 records through the admin path — ALTERNATIVES: a one-clause anchor on the §15.1 POST row (keeps a required field with no consumer that breaks the seeds); optional `command` on the payload (second argv source that can diverge from the CRD).
DECISION: `command`, the supervisor, and `restartable` are scoped to `type: agent` sidecar runtimes; `restartable` = carries `command`, `executionMode: session`, no `capabilities.preConnect: true` (§5.1 **`command`** is its one home) — BECAUSE no spec surface launches a `type: mcp` server from a sidecar runtime container and its runtime-side path is Phase 12b (spec/18_build-sequence.md:516 §18.29); one predicate excludes embedded, `type: mcp`, preConnect, service-mode, and admin-only records — ALTERNATIVES: a warm-up launch point for `type: mcp` (no cross-container transport; adapter drives MCP over stdio in pkg/adapter/mcpruntime.go).
DECISION: SPEC-2 **Environment.** is the one home of environment construction (fixed PATH, `env` whose PATH pair replaces the fixed one, platform variables; image ENTRYPOINT/CMD/ENV unused); SPEC-1 **`env`** points at it and keeps only field properties, including the not-a-secrets-channel sentence.
WATCHOUT: "In the sidecar model ..." sentences in SPEC-3 step 4, SPEC-5(c)-(e), SPEC-8 and SPEC-9 were deliberately NOT scoped to `type: agent`; they sit in text the spec already frames for `type: agent` (§4.7.9 title, spec/04 runtime-process paragraph). Only sentences stating where the supervisor exists were rescoped (SPEC-2 intro, SPEC-6, SPEC-7 intro/diagram/Endpoint, summary line on the supervisor, D-SCOPE, D-CLEANUP). Do not add `type: agent` to the rest — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:28 (`type: mcp` has no `capabilities`).
WATCHOUT: `type` is a required enum on RuntimeSpec (pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go:15-17), so the CODE-1 CEL rule reads "type is agent and deploymentModel is empty or sidecar"; do not write "empty defaults to each".
FACT: The only `kind: Runtime` CRD examples in spec/ are spec/17_deployment-topology.md:309 (registered via `lenny-ctl runtime register`, admin path) and :334 (`runtime-crds.yaml`, `deploymentModel: sidecar`); only the latter needs `command` (SPEC-1(d)) — EVIDENCE: grep -n 'kind: Runtime$' spec/*.md.

CORRECTS [ledger FACT on plan §10.3 admission-predicate vs supervisor-scope]: the proposal now follows the CRD-only decision (D-COMMAND), not the supervisor-scope decision.

### [spec.1.fix-G7.1]
DECISION: Exit Codes rows 1 and 137 lose their outcome clauses (`adapter logs stderr; `, ` — adapter treats as crash, pod is not reused`) rather than being scoped — BECAUSE the crash outcome already has homes (SPEC-2 **Crash.**, §5.2 "Runtime not live", the sentence under the table); a scoped row 137 is a third copy and still misreads a zero-grace boundary `end` on a restart pod — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "while a session is bound and not by an `end` the adapter requested" scoping; replacing row 1 with a stderr sentence (§15.4.1 is the stderr home after SPEC-8(d)).
DECISION: no embedded clause on the SPEC-8(c) RUNTIME_CRASH replacements — BECAUSE in the embedded model the runtime process is the adapter process (spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.10), so no surviving adapter synthesizes anything — ALTERNATIVES: an explicit embedded clause per occurrence, describing a case that cannot occur.
DECISION: §15.4 SIGKILL sites use the passive "the process is sent SIGKILL" with a §4.7.10 pointer only on the DemoteSDK paragraph — BECAUSE "the adapter sends SIGKILL" would make an implementor build a cross-container kill from the adapter UID.

No spec, docs, or test file outside §29.2 cites a §29.2 step >= 25 (the §29.3 table cites steps 15 through 20; tests cite only step 8) — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:288,337,353,390,578; tests/tier11_docs/concurrent_slot_lifecycle_doc_reconciliation_test.go:458
FACT: spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.9 step 7 ("connections the runtime opened on the pod's first session") is already replaced wholesale by SPEC-3(b); no SPEC-12 edit needed there.

SPEC-12 belongs in S6 beside SPEC-7 and SPEC-8 (S6 should read "SPEC-7, SPEC-8, and SPEC-12"); SPEC-13 belongs with S3 (SPEC-4 item 8 now links §24.2); SPEC-14 belongs in a spec step after S4 (S6 is suitable).
OPEN: docs/api/internal.md still says the adapter logs runtime stderr and sends SIGKILL; DOC-1's site list omits that page. A non-spec-lens finding for the next loop.
OPEN: summary D-PROPERTIES and SPEC-4 item 8 both say the self-test re-runs on a container-runtime version change "as §4.7.11 / item 1 requires for SO_PEERCRED"; item 1 states no such re-run (spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.11 item 1). Left for a later round per the G7 design.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Launch.** is the one home of the launch points; SPEC-3(b) step 7, SPEC-9, and SPEC-7(d) Preconditions point to it. SPEC-5(c) is the one home of the restart-boundary order; SPEC-2 **End.** and SPEC-7(d) point to it — BECAUSE the three copies already disagreed (SPEC-7 put `sweep` on `DemoteSDK`) — ALTERNATIVES: making the copies agree (rejected: next edit re-diverges them).
DECISION: `DemoteSDK` sends `end` only, no `sweep` — BECAUSE demotion runs at claim time before the session's workspace is materialized, `preConnect` cannot be `restart`, and on `keep` cross-session residue is what the acknowledgment covers; a sweep there only adds a deletion during a claim — ALTERNATIVES: end+sweep (non-spec CODE-3's old text; rejected).
DECISION: the launch gate is scoped to the `restart` boundary and the "never on a pod carrying a `scrub_warning`" clause is dropped — BECAUSE under `onScrubFailure: warn` the pod serves the next session (spec/05 onScrubFailure `warn` row; spec/06 **preConnect re-warm on scrub_warning.**), failed end/sweep already retire through "Runtime not live", and `scrub.Run` runs steps 1-6 whatever the cleanup result (pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go `Run`, comment "Steps 1-6 run regardless of a cleanup failure") — ALTERNATIVES: retire every `restart` pod on any scrub failure (rejected: a second exception to `onScrubFailure`, i.e. hair).
DECISION: the SDK re-warm edge launches nothing. After a demoted session on a `keep` pod the step-7 process is live and is the SDK-warm process; the re-warm launch SPEC-9 named never had an end preceding it — ALTERNATIVES: end-and-relaunch at re-warm (rejected: ends a kept process on a `keep` pod, and nothing stages that end).
DECISION: ping runs only while no other request is outstanding, and every request is bounded by the deadline of the operation that sends it; an unanswered request is a link failure — BECAUSE the supervisor is single-threaded C and serving `ping` during an `end` grace wait would need an event loop — ALTERNATIVES: exempt `ping` from the one-outstanding rule (rejected for that reason).
FACT: the tree already re-arms the intra-pod MCP surface on every start that holds the pod alone. `claimPodMCPStartLocked` (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go) returns the stale surface's cancels, and `StartSession` arms `startPlatformMCP(nonce)` with the new manifest's nonce before `Runtime.Start` (pkg/adapter/session.go). So a process relaunched on a `restart` pod authenticates to MCP in code, and D-FIXTURE's tier-5 MCP call holds — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go claimPodMCPStartLocked, takePodMCPCancelsLocked; pkg/adapter/session.go startPlatformMCP call
WATCHOUT: the spec's "pod-wide and started at most once per pod" (§4.7.6 `mcpNonce` row, §15.4.3 Authentication, §28.5.3 MCP cards) diverges from that takeover. This is a pre-existing spec-vs-code finding owned by 0084, which keeps the takeover. 0087 must not stage a fix for it — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md `mcpNonce` row; proposals/0084*/summary.md non-goal "Dropping the stale-surface takeover"
OPEN: on a `keep` `preConnect` pod (or a non-recycling one), a `DemoteSDK` relaunch makes D-TRANSPORT ("one `SocketRuntimeProcess` for the pod's life"), D-COHORT ("generation is 1 for the pod's life"), and CODE-3's `SupervisedRuntime` keep-pod composition false. This is a separate finding for a later round.
OPEN: under the SDK-warm circuit breaker (`sdkWarmDisabled`), a `preConnect` pod warms pod-warm and launches nothing at warm-up. SPEC-2 **Launch.** and SPEC-3(a) state the warm-up launch unconditionally. This is a separate finding.
WATCHOUT: docs/reference/adapter-contract.md says "before spawning your binary" twice; DOC-1 owns it — EVIDENCE: docs/reference/adapter-contract.md (two "before spawning your binary" sentences)

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** is the one home of the runtime-not-live trigger list (failed launch/end/sweep or CH-SUPERVISE failure; exit other than by an `end` while bound or on a pod that keeps its process; heartbeat escalation on a keep pod). SPEC-7 Degradation, the §28.8 CH-SUPERVISE row, SPEC-5(c), the summary, and the non-spec state table cite it — BECAUSE three copies disagreed on end/sweep scope — ALTERNATIVES: aligning the copies (rejected: they drift again).
DECISION: Relaunch is gated by a launch permit: a launch happens only when the pod has launched no process since its creation or its last attested end; a session that finds no live process and no permit fails its start (§5.2 "Runtime not live" already gives that outcome) — BECAUSE "when no process is live" relaunched a keep pod's idle-exited process and a concurrent pod after a crash — ALTERNATIVES: per-lifetime launch rules (more text, two predicates).
DECISION: Service-mode not-live = the adapter exits non-zero and the pod is replaced — BECAUSE agent pods use RestartPolicy Never, so adapter exit -> CH-SUPERVISE close -> supervisor exit -> pod Failed, and the WarmPoolController retires an idle Sandbox whose pod failed — ALTERNATIVES: "pod not ready" (D-SERVICE as written) has no replacer: nothing acts on a Running NotReady pod.
FACT: Agent pods are RestartPolicy Never — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37. lifecycle.Decide leaves Idle+PodNotReady alone and drains Idle+PodFailed — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:87-97.
FACT: CODE-2 already sends `exited` only for an exit outside an `end` (non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 `exited` bullet); the spec card did not say so.
UNVERIFIED: that service-mode pods sit in Sandbox phase `idle` so the Idle+PodFailed arm replaces them. The CODE-3 implementor should confirm.
WATCHOUT: G1 edits the SPEC-2(b) Launch bullet first. Apply the launch-permit replacement to whatever G1 left, and keep G1's post-end scrub ordering sentence.
OPEN: a link failure (missed pong, closed connection) states no outcome for bound sessions in the staged spec, while D-PING and edge row 37 say they fail. This is a separate finding.
OPEN: SPEC-3(b) step 7 said every preConnect session skips step 7, which contradicts D-SDKWARM's relaunch after a demotion. The G2 rewrite of step 7 into a pointer removes that clause.
OPEN: whether a runtime that exits voluntarily after a CH-MSGSOCK `shutdown` while sessions are bound should count as a crash. This belongs to R16 and the adapter shutdown proposal.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: `CH-SUPERVISE` authority direction is `Both` (register row and card Axes "message authority on both sides") — BECAUSE §28.2 defines the axis as the originator of messages and `exited` is unsolicited from the supervisor; this closes the review-log open item "the §28.3 authority direction for `exited`" — ALTERNATIVES: keeping `Pod adapter` and recasting `exited` as a reply to an outstanding request (rejected: it is sent outside any request, and CH-RUNTIMEOPS already sets the `Both` precedent). EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:80-81, :124-125, :562
DECISION: the `CH-SUPERVISE` card's **Exclusivity.** bullet is the home of the one-connection guards; the §28.6 **One connection per pod.** paragraph and the §28.8 row's exclusivity cell point at it — BECAUSE §28.6's opening says each card states the constraint and guard and §28.6 "states no constraint the cards do not carry", the §28.2 Exclusivity axis says per-channel values are stated with the cards, and the proposal's own home table gives exclusivity to the card — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's suggestion to make §28.6 the home (rejected: it inverts the §28.6 structure the existing spec uses).
DECISION: the supervisor repeats a refused dial until a connection is made, and exits when that connection closes. "Never dials a second time" is withdrawn. The guards become listener-close plus exit-on-close — BECAUSE the adapter and runtime are both regular containers, the adapter has no postStart hook, and kubelet does not wait for the adapter to bind, so a single dial loses the race under RestartPolicy Never — ALTERNATIVES: a postStart hook or native-sidecar ordering for the adapter (rejected: new pod mechanism, and the adapter's READY waits on the supervisor, so a startup-probe gate would deadlock); reversing the dial direction (rejected: it moves the same race to the adapter); a supervisor-side retry deadline (rejected: a timing heuristic, and the adapter's wait already fails the pod closed).
FACT: the agent pod's containers are `adapter` then `runtime`, both regular containers with no postStart hook, under `RestartPolicy: Never`. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:75-104
WATCHOUT: retry only on a refused connect (`ECONNREFUSED`). A connection that is accepted and then fails the supervisor's adapter-UID check must still exit and must not be retried, or a squatter at another UID would get repeated attempts. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 `run` mode
WATCHOUT: the code-best-practices rule on overridable non-spec constants applies to the supervisor's dial retry interval. Staged text leaves the value to the implementor, and the card's Timing bullet lists it among the bounds the spec does not state.
UNVERIFIED: when the adapter exits non-zero on its wait, the runtime container keeps retrying its dial, so the pod stays Running and not Ready rather than Failed. The tree behaves the same today with any runtime entrypoint. Whether the WarmPoolController or the GC reaps such a pod is unchecked. The owner of a pod-reaping lens should check it.
DECISION: the SPEC-6 mount rule becomes "no container other than the init container `lenny-supervisor-stage` mounts it read-write", following the credential-volume precedent in §13.1, which names containers. The volume's name is left out of the spec and stays an implementation constant shared by `applySupervisor` and `pkg/podsecurity`. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:28

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** is the one home of how the supervisor runs `cleanupCommands` (UID, forked worker, working directory, environment, aggregate bound); SPEC-5(c) keeps only which component runs them per model (supervisor in sidecar, adapter in embedded) and cites **Runtime supervisor.**; SPEC-4 **Protection from author code.** stays the one home of "no forked worker holds a CH-SUPERVISE descriptor", so the Cleanup bullet drops that clause — BECAUSE SPEC-4's sentence is the general protection property (it also covers the sweep worker, which SPEC-2 has no bullet for) — ALTERNATIVES: cutting SPEC-4's cleanup mention and keeping the descriptor clause in SPEC-2 (loses the property from the trust argument that rests on it).
DECISION: delete the SPEC-5(c) Recycle-lifecycle parenthetical edit outright — BECAUSE the tree sentence "cleanupCommands execute in the pod's workspace base /workspace" names no executor and stays true; the parenthetical only restated the UID rule.
FACT: the tree bounds cleanupCommands in aggregate, not per command — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go:132-134 (CleanupTimeout "bounds the whole cleanupCommands phase"), :310; spec/05 §5.2 Lenny scrub procedure (gateway missing-report timeout = cleanupTimeoutSeconds plus grace). SPEC-7 Messages and non-spec `cleanup` bullet (timeoutMs deadline) were already aggregate; only SPEC-2 Cleanup and edge row :42 said per-command.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2 has no **Sweep.** bullet; the sweep's mechanism is stated only in SPEC-5(c)/(e) while the home table gives SPEC-2 "when it launches and ends". Not touched by G4; a later round may file it.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]

DECISION: SPEC-5(b) defines the term "reusing recycling pool" (recycle.enabled, maxSessionsPerPod > 1, scrubProfile not vm-restart) once; the acknowledgment rule, the gateway reuse predicate, the YAML comment, and summary D-LIFETIME cite it — BECAUSE the triple was written three times and the YAML comment's "where the acknowledgment rule applies" became circular once SPEC-5(g) added `keep` to that rule — ALTERNATIVES: defining the term in the acknowledgment paragraph (rejected: SPEC-5(b) is where runtimeProcess is required, and the acknowledgment paragraph sits below it).
DECISION: Every reserved-hold and drain site outside the acknowledgment paragraph (§4.6.1 SandboxClaim row and **Reserved hold**, §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row, §5.2 pinned-idle exit list, §6.2 diagram and **`reserved` hold semantics**, §29.2 step 5) cites the per-pod refusal ("when an acquisition refuses the pod under the runtime-process acknowledgment") instead of a pool predicate — BECAUSE after 0087 the refusal is per pod (a keep-created pod on a restart pool), so no pool-level predicate is correct — ALTERNATIVES: "on a pool that does not reuse pods" (rejected: misses the keep-created used pod after an edit to restart).
DECISION: The pod-lifetime carrier is stated in SPEC-5(b): WarmPoolController writes `lenny.dev/runtime-process` at creation, the label joins the §17.2 item 5 immutable list, acquisitions read it with the tenant pin, absent or unknown reads as `keep` — BECAUSE the gateway SA holds pod patch, and the label gates a fail-closed reuse refusal — ALTERNATIVES: reading the adapter's `--runtime-process` arg from the immutable pod spec (rejected: no acquisition reads container args today, and the label read already sits beside the pin read); a mirror column (rejected: the fallback claim reads the live pod through AdmitTenantPin, not the mirror).
DECISION: The `restartable` gate gains one render-time sentence (WarmPoolController renders `restart` only for a restartable runtime, else creates no pod), following the nonce-only render precedent in §5.3 **`acknowledgeNonceOnlyAuth`** — ALTERNATIVES: rendering `keep` instead (rejected: fails open, because a restart pool may lack acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation); Runtime-edit impact validation (rejected: a direct CRD apply bypasses it).
FACT: The Postgres-backed fallback claim reads the live pod's labels through `podclaim.AdmitTenantPin`, not the `agent_pod_state` mirror; only drain stamping uses mirror state — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go:168
FACT: `lenny-label-immutability` refuses every change of a strictly-immutable label on UPDATE, including unset to value, so adding a key to its list also blocks a later add — EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:132-150
WATCHOUT: The §4.6.3 SandboxClaim ownership row is pinned byte-exact by `claimOwnershipRowSentence`; any edit to that row must update the constant in the same step — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_register_writers_test.go:103-117
WATCHOUT: Proposal 0096 (skeleton, nothing staged) will rewrite the allowCrossTenantReuse paragraph; 0087 changes only the rationale clauses there, and 0096 must stage against the post-0087 text — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:457
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: S4 says SPEC-5 "lands ... in §5.2"; after this round SPEC-5 also edits §5.1 (example YAML), §4.6.1, §4.6.3, §6.2, §17.2 item 5, and §29.2. S4 should name those sections.
OPEN: DOC-1 does not list the docs pages that justify the pin with "a recycled pod keeps the runtime process that served its pinned tenant" (docs/operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md, namespace-and-isolation.md, installation.md, docs/reference/execution-modes.md); false under `restart`. A later round should add them to DOC-1.
OPEN: Go doc comments still name the old pool predicate ("configuration keeps no runtime process across sessions") at pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandboxclaim_types.go:63 (CRD YAML regenerated from it), and in podclaim/tenantpin.go and claimer.go; CODE-5 does not list them.
FACT: The §16 `LabelImmutabilityWebhookUnavailable` description already omits `lenny.dev/dns-policy`, so its label list is not exhaustive; adding `lenny.dev/runtime-process` there is not required — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:493
UNVERIFIED: Whether podspec `Inputs` already carries the runtime's `capabilities.preConnect`, which the render-time restartable guard needs; CODE-4's implementor should check.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]

DECISION: `command` and `env` are settable only on the Runtime CRD, as `requireSoPeercred` is (§5.1 **`requireSoPeercred`**); SPEC-10 and the admin-payload half of CODE-1 are deleted, and `restartable` is derived only by the RuntimeReconciler from the CRD, so an admin-only record is not restartable and `restart` is refused on it (fail-closed) — BECAUSE the supervisor-scope consequence "admin registration API gains `command`" rests on "admin-registered runtimes are all sidecars", which the ledger already records as stale, the admission-predicate decision of the same day says `command` stays CRD-only, the pod builder reads only the live CRD, and requiring `command` on the admin payload breaks every bootstrap seed (`lenny up` seeds the embedded `echo` and the §26 records through `/v1/admin/bootstrap`, §17.6 `runtimes:` uses the admin schema) — ALTERNATIVES: optional `command` on the admin payload (a second source of the argv that can diverge from the CRD the pod builder reads; hair); keep it required and add `command` to every admin-path example and seed (forces a field nobody reads, and the embedded echo seed cannot carry it).
DECISION: the supervisor, `command`, and `restartable` are scoped to `type: agent` sidecar runtimes; `restartable` is defined as "carries `command`, session-mode, not `preConnect`", which excludes embedded, `type: mcp`, and admin-only records in one predicate — BECAUSE the spec defines no `type: mcp` pod launch (its runtime-side path is Phase 12b, §18.29) and the tree's `MCPRuntime` (pkg/adapter/mcpruntime.go) spawns the MCP server from the adapter over stdio, which no supervisor launch can feed — ALTERNATIVES: a warm-up launch point for `type: mcp` (needs a stdio transport across containers that does not exist; Phase 12b's design).
FACT: §15.1 has no field-level description of the admin runtime payload; only the endpoint rows and the dry-run validation row mention `/v1/admin/runtimes` — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:803-810, :1223
FACT: `lenny up` registers echo through the admin bootstrap and applies an embedded echo Runtime CRD separately — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/runtimes.go:34-40, :63-69
FACT: The §15.4 sites SPEC-8(c) told a "review run" to check stay true under SPEC-8(a): the drain row says "SIGTERM on timeout" and the Basic conformance row says the adapter will SIGKILL — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1785, :2071. The `RUNTIME_CRASH` sites describe a process exit, which the embedded model's in-process loop does not have, so no embedded clause is owed — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:596-600
FACT: The admin PUT path merges only the fields the body sets onto the stored record, so a CRD-mirrored `restartable` survives an admin update — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/runtimes.go:982-1029
WATCHOUT: the "In the sidecar model" sentences of SPEC-5, SPEC-3(a), SPEC-8, and SPEC-9 inherit the existing spec's implicit `type: agent` framing (§4.7.10 ends "The contract covers `type: agent` runtimes"); do not sprinkle `type: agent` into each. Only sentences that state where the supervisor exists ("every sidecar pod") restate the scope and were retargeted — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:982
UNVERIFIED: whether a derived runtime's registry record inherits `restartable` from its base; CODE-1 does not say. A derived pool's `restart` is refused if it does not (fail-closed). CODE-1's owner should state it.
OPEN: the registry `command` and `env` columns have no reader other than the derived-runtime merge once the admin payload is gone; a later round may decide whether to mirror only `restartable`.
OPEN: §26.3 "**Setup-time image entrypoint:**" and its lifecycle steps predate the deployment-model split and do not state the reference runtime's deployment model; a separate finding, not retargeted here.
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: S1 reads "SPEC-1 and SPEC-10. Lands `command` and `env` in §5.1 and on the §15.1 admin payload."; SPEC-10 is deleted, so S1 is "SPEC-1. Lands `command` and `env` in §5.1 and the §17.4 example." S7 reads "every existing sidecar fixture and bootstrap runtime"; bootstrap records are admin-path and gain nothing, so it is "every existing sidecar fixture Runtime CRD".

### [spec.1.fix-design-G7.1]

DECISION: Exit Codes rows 1 and 137 are closed by DELETION ("adapter logs stderr; " and " — adapter treats as crash, pod is not reused"), not by scoping clauses — BECAUSE the outcome of an exit already has its homes (§4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Crash.**, §5.2 "Runtime not live", and the sentence under the table about a non-zero exit during an active session); a scoped row 137 would be a third statement of the crash predicate — ALTERNATIVES: "while a session is bound and not by an `end` the adapter requested" (rejected: restates **Crash.** and misses the `keep` no-session exit, which §5.2 retires).
DECISION: No embedded clause on the `RUNTIME_CRASH` replacements — BECAUSE in the embedded model the runtime process is the adapter process (spec/04_system-components.md:959), so "the process exits non-zero" with a surviving adapter only exists in the sidecar model. SPEC-8(c)'s "review run" sentences are deleted and replaced by staged §15.4 edits (SPEC-8(d)).
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]: its FACT that the §15.4 Basic conformance row (:2071) "stays true" — the adapter (adapter UID, separate container) cannot signal the agent-UID process; the supervisor does. §15.4 DemoteSDK contract (:1470) has the same defect. Both are replaced in place by SPEC-8(d). The drain row (:1785, "SIGTERM on timeout") does stay true and is not edited.
DECISION: §29.2 gains a `runtime supervisor` participant (§29.1 table) and a new step 25 (`adapter` → `runtime supervisor`, `CH-SUPERVISE`, launch); steps 25–34 renumber to 26–35 — BECAUSE §29.1 forbids letter suffixes for ordered steps and requires every channel a trace uses to appear. In-section step citations to renumber: "Steps 24 through 31" (step 23, step 32, step 34) → 32; "26a, 26b, and 26c" → 27a–27c; "(step 25)" in old 29 → 26; "as step 31 states" in old 34 → 32. No citation of a §29.2 step number exists outside spec/29 (grep of spec/ and docs/ on 2026-10-22).
DECISION: The §29.2 "On the first session of a pod-warm pod" conditions (old steps 25, 26a, 26b, 26c, 27, 28) become "On the first session of the runtime process of a pod-warm pod" — BECAUSE that phrase is true under `keep`, `restart`, and the embedded model (whose runtime process is the adapter process) without a lifetime conditional.
WATCHOUT: §29.2 step 7's hold predicate is a reserved-hold site G5 retargets to the per-pod refusal; G5's shard lists it as "§29.2 step 5", which carries no predicate — the site is step 7 — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:174-178
FACT: §4.7.11 item 1 states no rule to re-run the `SO_PEERCRED` self-test when the container runtime version changes; it runs on every pod start — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:994-1001. SPEC-4 item 8's "as item 1 requires for `SO_PEERCRED`" and summary D-PROPERTIES's "as §4.7.11 requires" cite a rule that does not exist (separate finding, not fixed by G7).
FACT: Phase 2's exit gate includes the startup-latency baseline in tests/tier7b_load_kind, which runs pods on Kind, so the supervisor, its image, and the stage init container belong in Phase 2 rather than Phase 3 — EVIDENCE: spec/18_build-sequence.md:126, :142
OPEN: docs/api/internal.md carries an "adapter logs stderr"/"adapter sends SIGKILL"-style sentence and DOC-1 does not list it (DOC-1 names only docs/reference/adapter-contract.md for the exit-code statements).
OPEN: §29.2 old step 26a says the intra-pod MCP servers start at most once per pod and a later manifest write does not re-arm the nonce; SPEC-2 **End.** closes and rebinds every runtime listener between generations, so under `restart` the server restarts per process. Not filed by this round.
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: no step lands SPEC-12 (spec/28 rule 1 and Basic trace, spec/29), SPEC-13 (§24.2), SPEC-14 (§18.6, §18.30), or SPEC-8(d) (§15.4). True instead: S6 lands "SPEC-7, SPEC-8, and SPEC-12" (depends on S2, S3, S4); S3 lands "SPEC-3, SPEC-4, SPEC-11, and SPEC-13"; SPEC-14 lands in S6 or a new spec step after S4.

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: `SupervisedRuntime` composes one `SocketRuntimeProcess` per launch on both lifetimes (CODE-3, D-TRANSPORT) — BECAUSE `SocketRuntimeProcess.CloseListener` sets sticky `ended` and closes the listener, so reusing the pod's single object after a `DemoteSDK` end makes every demoted session's start fail with `errRuntimeConnectionEnded` — ALTERNATIVES: a reset method on `SocketRuntimeProcess` (rejected: breaks 0079's sticky-ended rule per object).
FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess.CloseListener` sets `tornDown` and `ended` and closes the listener; the ended state is sticky by design — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go errRuntimeConnectionEnded doc comment and CloseListener
DECISION: the supervisor's refused-dial retry is bounded (SPEC-7 **Timing.**; CODE-2 `--dial-timeout`, default at least the adapter's `AwaitSupervisor` timeout) — BECAUSE an adapter that dies before binding @lenny-supervise would otherwise leave the runtime container running forever, the pod Running and not Ready under RestartPolicy Never, and lifecycle.Decide takes no action on Warming/PodNotReady, so the Sandbox never leaves warming — ALTERNATIVES: a controller warming deadline (out of scope; no such deadline exists in pkg/controller/sandbox).
WATCHOUT: CODE-4's rendered `run` command does not pass `--dial-timeout`; the supervisor uses the flag default. If a later loop wants it chart-tunable, CODE-4 must render it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 `applySupervisor` bullet
FACT: the label-immutability set is enumerated at three spec sites: §4.6.1 host-schedulability paragraph ("... only"), the §13.2 **Label immutability note** (physically inside §13.2.3), and §17.2 item 5. SPEC-5(h) now edits all three — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1; spec/13_security-model.md "Label immutability note"; spec/17_deployment-topology.md item 5
DECISION: step 7's skip sentence now covers only "a session that starts while a process is live"; the no-process, no-permit case is left to **Launch.**'s failed start.
DECISION: SPEC-9 cites **Runtime supervisor.** for the warm-up start; "Runtime process lifetime" does not state when the process starts.
OPEN: D-SCOPE (`type: agent` only) and D-COMMAND (CRD-only `command`) depart from the problem statement's "Owner decisions this proposal implements" (every sidecar pod runs the supervisor; `command` on the admin API). Both summary entries now say so and need owner sign-off. Narrowing the problem statement's record is a reframe this lane may not make; the introspection pass or the owner decides.
CORRECTS [G6 fix]: it narrowed scope to `type: agent` but left the summary goal, CODE-3's `--supervise-socket` description ("empty only in the embedded model"), and D-STAGE's "no regular container" mount rule stating the old scope; all three repaired.

### [spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1]

FACT: Two live sentences assert `DemoteSDK` does not end the runtime process: §4.7.10 **Runtime process lifetime.** ("distinct from the runtime process, and neither ends the runtime process") and the §28.5.3 session-frame-writes table row for a refused second confirmation ("because `DemoteSDK` does not end the runtime process"). SPEC-2 End and SPEC-9 make `DemoteSDK` end the sidecar process, and no staged edit touches either sentence. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:960-963, spec/28_communication-channels.md:692

DECISION: deleted the SPEC-1(b) **`env`** clause "so a runtime registered only through the admin API is not `restartable`" and SPEC-5(b)'s "in the runtime registry" — BECAUSE the SPEC-1(b) **`command`** predicate alone decides every case: a derived runtime inherits command/executionMode/capabilities so is restartable iff its base is, and a standalone admin-only record has no command so is not — ALTERNATIVES: "standalone/derived" qualifiers plus a `restartable` merge-table row (rejected: restartable is computed, not a definition field); dropping the mirrored column (rejected: plan §10.3 mirrored-flag mechanism, D-RESTARTABLE); declaring derived runtimes non-restartable (contradicts the predicate).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: The tree's runtime-liveness predicate is `connected && !ended` on the one CH-MSGSOCK connection (SocketRuntimeProcess.ServesNextSession), so a connection ended for any reason (runtime close, hold-timeout CloseListener, exit) already reads not live — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-459; spec/04_system-components.md:695 (`ReportPodScrub` row).
DECISION: Liveness gains one keep-scoped condition, "its CH-MSGSOCK connection ended", merged with the existing keep-scoped exit and hung clauses into one clause — BECAUSE it restores the tree's own keep predicate and covers the hold-timeout closure without naming the hold timeout; the SPEC-2(a) hold-timeout sentence then loses its sidecar half so Liveness is the one home — ALTERNATIVES: listing "the hold timeout closed the connection" as its own trigger (narrower, misses a runtime-initiated close); having the hold timeout send `end` (makes a keep pod relaunch under **Launch.**, a new mechanism).
DECISION: The §4.7.1 `ReportPodScrub` row's own predicate is replaced by a pointer to **Liveness.** rather than reworded — BECAUSE two predicates for one proto field (`runtime_live`) is the defect; on `restart` no process exists after the scrub, so the row says "runtime" rather than "runtime process".
DECISION: Missed pong / frozen supervisor does not fail bound sessions; edge row and D-PING lose that clause — BECAUSE a frozen supervisor does not stop the runtime, and Liveness retires at the boundary; a killed supervisor ends the container's processes as namespace init, so sessions fail through CH-MSGSOCK closing anyway.
DECISION: The agent pod's `restartPolicy: Never` is stated once, as the first guard in the CH-SUPERVISE card **Exclusivity.**; §4.7.11 item 1 step 4 and §10.3 lose their `CrashLoopBackOff` claims — BECAUSE the code already renders Never and the one-connection and author-code-free ordering both rest on it — ALTERNATIVES: a §13.1 pod-spec rule (needs webhook enforcement design, out of scope); restating it in SPEC-4 item 8 too (second home).
WATCHOUT: CODE-3's `ServesNextSession()` must not be `!notLive` alone on a keep pod; the composed SocketRuntimeProcess's ended state is the Liveness connection condition — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 "Adapter state for one pod".

S6 says SPEC-7 lands CH-SUPERVISE in §28; after this round SPEC-7(h) also edits §4.7.11 item 1 step 4 and §10.3. Each step should name its deliverable only (pointer), not list rows.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]

DECISION: The §5.2 sessionPolicy reference block keeps `runtimeProcess` as a commented line carrying only the pointer comment — BECAUSE the block has `recycle.enabled: false`, so any present value is rejected by SPEC-5(b), and the rule's one home is SPEC-5(b) — ALTERNATIVES: delete the line (leaves the field reference block without the field; a likely next-round completeness finding); the reviewer's long comment restating "required/rejected/no default" (a second statement of SPEC-5(b), which round 1 removed).
DECISION: The derived-runtime `restartable` contradiction is closed by DELETING the consequence clause "so a runtime registered only through the admin API is not `restartable`" from SPEC-1(b) `env`, not by narrowing it — BECAUSE the predicate in SPEC-1(b) `command` already decides every case once `command`, `executionMode`, and `capabilities` are inherited (SPEC-1(c), §5.1 merge table rows `executionMode`, `capabilities.*`) — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "standalone ... and a derived runtime takes it from its base" (enumerates exceptions to a failed claim); a `restartable` row in the merge table (it is a computed value, not a Runtime definition field; hair).
FACT: Gateway pool admission reads the UNRESOLVED runtime record (`r.runtimes.Get`, not `runtimestore.Resolve`) for preConnect, multi-turn, workspace tier, and requireSoPeercred, so a derived runtime's pool is checked against fields the derived record may not set — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:606-617; pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/merge.go:15-29 (Resolve), :62-76 (Merge copies Capabilities, RequireSoPeercred from base).
WATCHOUT: `restartable` is a column the RuntimeReconciler computes from the CRD; a derived record is admin-registered and never mirrored, so unless `runtimestore.Merge` copies `Restartable` from the base AND the restart gate reads the resolved runtime, every derived pool's `restart` is refused — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/merge.go:62-76; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:606.
CORRECTS [review-log UNVERIFIED line "whether a derived runtime's registry record inherits `restartable` from its base"]: settled — by the SPEC-1(b) predicate a derived runtime is restartable exactly when its base is; CODE-1 now has `Merge` take `Restartable` from the base and CODE-5's gate reads the resolved runtime.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5(b) stops locating the lifetime-label read ("where it reads the tenant pin") and keeps only the decoding rule (absent or unknown reads as `keep`); the §5.2 acknowledgment paragraph, which already says "every acquisition refuses such a pod, including the `reserved → bound` rebind and the Postgres-backed fallback claim", is the one home for which acquisitions refuse. CODE-5 adds the label read to `Claimer.rebindReserved` through one helper shared with `AdmitTenantPin`. BECAUSE the defect was a location claim in (b) duplicating scope that (g) owns; deleting it differs in kind from enumerating the rebind into it. ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "Every acquisition, including the rebind, reads ... before it binds" (a second, narrower statement of scope (g) owns; the next round finds the next unenumerated path); mirroring the lifetime onto a Sandbox label so the rebind's existing list carries it (new surface, a second writer of the same fact).
FACT: The rebind reads no Pod: `rebindReserved` walks the Sandbox list, finds the tenant's reserved claim via `reservedClaimForTenant`, and patches with `WriteRebindStatus`; the only pod-level gate today is the pool-level `!req.KeepsRuntime` → `endReservedHolds` branch in `Claimer.Claim`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:154-161, :302-320.
FACT: The current §5.2 acknowledgment paragraph already names the rebind as an acquisition the rule refuses, and SPEC-5(g)'s prefix replacement preserves that clause. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:559 ("including the `reserved → bound` rebind").
WATCHOUT: After the rebind ends a refused hold, the pod returns to `idle` pinned; the same acquisition read its Sandbox as `reserved`, so it stamps no drain request; the next idle scan's `AdmitTenantPin` refusal plus `DrainRefusedPod` does. Do not add a drain stamp to the rebind path.

So the SPEC-2 **Launch.** sentence "a session that starts while a process is live uses that process" opens no path to the stale SDK-warm process.

### [spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]

DECISION: SPEC-2(a)'s third Find/Replace becomes a deletion of the whole tree sentence `A runtime process that stops, or whose connection the hold timeout closed, ... and the pod is retired ([§5.2](...)).` — BECAUSE the sidecar case has its one home in **Runtime supervisor.** **Liveness.**, and in the embedded model the runtime process is the adapter, so no reporter survives its stop; rounds 1 and 2 narrowed the sentence, and each narrowing was the next finding — ALTERNATIVES: rescoping again (the same answer one step smaller); stating an embedded outcome (pod exit under RestartPolicy Never needs no spec sentence here).
DECISION: spec/10 §10.1 **Hold state timeout:** stops asserting the pod's outcome and points to §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Liveness.** — BECAUSE Liveness already retires a `keep` pod whose CH-MSGSOCK connection ended, and a `restart` pod's process is ended by the boundary's `end` before the next session, so the credential concern the §10.1 clause guards does not arise there — ALTERNATIVES: adding "the coordinator hold timed out" as an unconditional Liveness trigger (rejected in round 2's G1.1 decision at review-log line 398; retires restart pods for no isolation gain); scoping §10.1 to keep pods (a second statement of the Liveness predicate).
