### [redesign.2026-10-22]

DECISION: The sidecar SDK-warm lifecycle has one home, a new SPEC-2 **Runtime supervisor.** **SDK-warm.** bullet, and §4.7.10 **Runtime process lifetime.** (both SPEC-2(a) citations), **End.**, SPEC-3(a), SPEC-8(d)1, SPEC-9(a), SPEC-9(c) (the §6.1 compatibility row), SPEC-9(d) (§6.2 **preConnect re-warm on scrub_warning.**), D-SDKWARM, D-LAUNCH, and CODE-3 cite it — BECAUSE three rounds repaired the lifecycle one sentence at a time across those sites, and the unedited compatibility row still said the adapter re-establishes SDK-warm state on the re-warm edge — ALTERNATIVES: a §6.1 home (rejected: §6.1 states both deployment models, and the supervisor's launch points live in §4.7.10).
DECISION: A pod launches its `preConnect` runtime at warm-up only when the pool's SDK-warm circuit breaker is closed at pod creation, fixed by the pod builder in the adapter flag `--warm-up-launch`; a pod created with the breaker open warms pod-warm with no process — BECAUSE the WarmPoolController already reads `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` per Sandbox when it picks the `sdk_connecting` route, §6.1 requires new pods to warm pod-warm while the breaker is open, and fixing the choice at creation follows D-PODLIFETIME — ALTERNATIVES: an unconditional warm-up launch (rejected: §6.1 says new pods warm pod-warm while the breaker is open, and the adapter would pay the demotion penalty the breaker exists to stop); the adapter reading the breaker live (rejected: a second reader of a controller-owned field and a launch decision that changes under a running pod). EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go `resolveSDKWarm`, `sdkWarmActive`; pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go `DecideSDKWarm` caller comment.
DECISION: The warm-up READY gate has one home, **Launch.**: the adapter signals READY only after the warm-up process has connected on `CH-MSGSOCK`, and a failed warm-up launch or a connect timeout exits the adapter non-zero before READY. It covers service-mode and `preConnect` runtimes alike — BECAUSE the gate is the same for both, and the breaker decision above means a pod without a warm-up launch has no gate to wait on.
DECISION: A `ConfigureWorkspace` that finds no live process launches one at step 7 under the **Launch.** permit — BECAUSE the gateway picks `ConfigureWorkspace` from the runtime's capability rather than the pod's state, and the tree's in-process SDK-warm runtime already starts the runtime there whether or not it pre-connected. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go (`req.PreConnect && !req.Demoted`); pkg/adapter/embedded_sdkwarm.go `ConfigureWorkspace`.
DECISION: `DemoteSDK` keeps 0090's order and fail-closed rule on a supervised pod: on a slot-guard timeout it sends no `end`, and an `end` error fails the request and marks the runtime not live — BECAUSE the §28.5.3 **Session frame writes.** row and 0090's decision 29 already fix that behavior, and **Liveness.** already counts a failed `end`.
DECISION: The re-warm edge launches nothing, and `PreConnect` runs once, at startup under `--warm-up-launch` — BECAUSE `preConnect` runtimes are never `restartable`, so the kept process is the SDK-warm process, and the gateway completes the re-warm on the pod's Ready condition, which the adapter never drops. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go (re-warm completion polls pod readiness).
DECISION: What the runtime sweep removes has one home, a new SPEC-2 **Runtime supervisor.** **Sweep.** bullet whose volume list is the runtime container's writable mounts (`/sessions`, `/artifacts`, `/tmp`, `/dev/shm`, and `/workspace` other than `slots/`); SPEC-5(c), (d), and (e), D-SWEEP, and CODE-2 cite it — BECAUSE SPEC-5 stated the reach three ways and the summary and CODE-2 a fourth — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go `runtimeMounts` (`/run/lenny` and `/workspace/shared` read-only); security_context.go `ReadOnlyRootFilesystem`.
DECISION: The sweep excludes the workspace base's `slots/` — BECAUSE scrub step 2 removes `slots/` after `cleanupCommands`, and the commands run with the workspace base as working directory and may read the ended sessions' trees; agent-planted symbolic links there give the commands nothing, because they run at the agent UID (D-CLEANUP) — ALTERNATIVES: sweeping `slots/` too (rejected: removes data the deployer's commands may read, for no isolation gain on a boundary that step 2 clears).
DECISION: D-SCOPE's `type: mcp` exclusion and D-COMMAND's CRD-only `command` moved into the summary's open decisions as owner sign-off items, each with the recorded owner decision, the departure, the reason, and a recommendation to approve. The staged design is unchanged.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1 OPEN "SPEC-3(a) gates READY on a `preConnect` runtime ... circuit breaker"]: settled. A pod created while the breaker is open has no warm-up launch and no warm-up READY gate (**SDK-warm.**, **Launch.**).
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN "under the SDK-warm circuit breaker (`sdkWarmDisabled`) ... launches nothing at warm-up"]: settled by the same decision.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G4.1 OPEN "SPEC-2 has no **Sweep.** bullet"]: settled; SPEC-2 now carries **Sweep.**, and the home table names it.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1 OPEN and spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN on one `SocketRuntimeProcess` for a `keep` pod's life]: already settled in the staging before this pass; D-TRANSPORT, D-COHORT, and CODE-3 compose one per launch, which a demoted relaunch gets.
CORRECTS [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1 OPEN "D-SCOPE ... and D-COMMAND ... need owner sign-off"]: moved to the summary's open decisions.
FACT: In the tree the circuit breaker reaches no adapter. Only the WarmPoolController's phase routing reads it, and the embedded `preconnect-echo` pre-connects at startup whatever its value, so §6.1's "new pods warm to `idle` in pod-warm mode only" does not hold for embedded pods. Recorded under the summary's defects not staged. EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go `PreConnect` goroutine; grep `SDKWarmDisabled` pkg/ (no adapter or gateway reader).
WATCHOUT: `SDKWarmReady` in pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go has no non-test caller; the READY gate is the adapter's `srv.Serve` ordering, not a readiness-probe hook.

### [redesign.2026-10-22.review]

FIX: The **Sweep.** volume list read as a list of exclusions ("the workspace base other than its `slots/` directory, `/sessions`, ..."). It now names the volumes first and the `slots/` exclusion last, in SPEC-2 and in the two review-log statements of the home.
FIX: CODE-3 claimed no deployment-boundary row because CODE-4 renders every new flag "in the same step", but CODE-3 lands at S9 and CODE-4 at S11, and nothing renders `--warm-up-timeout`. CODE-3 now adds `deferred` rows with blocker CODE-4, which CODE-4 deletes, and a `deployment-boundary-defaults.yaml` row for `--warm-up-timeout`. — EVIDENCE: tests/registers/exceptions-deployment-boundary.yaml header; tests/registers/deployment-boundary-defaults.yaml header.
OPEN: On a `restart` boundary the order is `end`, `sweep`, `cleanup`, a second `end`, and steps 1 to 6, with no second sweep. Anything the `cleanupCommands` create at the agent UID after the sweep (System V and POSIX IPC objects, and entries in `/sessions`, `/artifacts`, and the workspace base outside `slots/`) persists into the next session's process; step 4 clears only `/tmp` and `/dev/shm`, and the staged step-1b sentence hands IPC to the sweep, whereas the embedded model's step 1b runs after the commands. Because the sweep leaves `slots/` in place, agent-planted files there (for example a repository configuration or a build file) can steer a deployer command into running agent-chosen code whose output survives. Symbolic links in `slots/` give no gain: the commands run at the agent UID, and the adapter's step 2 and step 6 resolve with `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` (D-PATHS). Candidate fix: a second `sweep` after the second `end`, or the whole sweep moved after it with `slots/` still excluded. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md **Lenny scrub procedure.** steps 1b and 4; summary D-END and D-CLEANUP order.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S5 reads "Lands §13.1 **Runtime supervisor volume.** and the §6.1 SDK-warm text"; SPEC-9 now also edits the §6.1 compatibility row and §6.2 **preConnect re-warm on scrub_warning.** (SPEC-9(c), (d)). S1 still names the deleted SPEC-10 (deferred earlier).
FACT: §15.4 **SDK-warm demotion contract:** gives a 10-second `DemoteSDK` window, while the §4.7 RPC table's `ConfigureWorkspace` row and the adapter's `DefaultDemoteTimeout` use 5 seconds. **SDK-warm.** bounds the grace by the request's deadline, so it does not depend on either value. The mismatch predates this proposal. — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md **SDK-warm demotion contract:**; pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go `DefaultDemoteTimeout`.

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: On a `restart` boundary the order is step 0, `end`, `sweep`, `cleanup`, a second `end` and `sweep`, then steps 1 to 6 (caller's delegated decision). Staged normatively in SPEC-5(c); SPEC-2 **Sweep.** now runs "after each attested end at the points" §5.2 names. Summary D-CLEANUP is the one summary statement of the order; D-END and D-SWEEP order sentences were deleted. BECAUSE the commands run at the agent UID after the first sweep, and step 4 and step 6 do not reach `/sessions`, `/artifacts`, the workspace base outside `slots/`, or IPC in the sidecar model. ALTERNATIVES: one sweep moved after the second end (rejected: the caller fixed both sweeps, and the first keeps agent residue out of the commands' environment); extending step 4 or 6 (rejected: second home for the reach list).
CORRECTS [review-log OPEN "On a `restart` boundary the order is `end`, `sweep`, `cleanup`, a second `end`, and steps 1 to 6, with no second sweep"]: closed. The second sweep is staged in SPEC-2 **Sweep.** and SPEC-5(c), and in non-spec CH-SUPERVISE trace, CODE-1 `cleanup`, CODE-3 `EndRuntimeAgain`, and the Tier 1 list. SPEC-5(d)'s step-1b append now holds unchanged.

### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5(b) §5.2 **Runtime-process lifetime** now has the WarmPoolController write `keep` on a pool without `recycle.runtimeProcess` — BECAUSE the field exists only on reusing recycling pools, so non-recycling, concurrent, single-session, and vm-restart pools had no lifetime and SPEC-2(a)'s §5.2 pointer did not back its keep clause — ALTERNATIVES: restoring "or a pool that does not recycle" in SPEC-2(a) (second home, and still omits concurrent/single-session/vm-restart pools); relying on the acquisition's absent-reads-as-keep sentence (governs the gateway read, not what the controller writes).
CORRECTS [review-log:630, :647]: the claim that the absent-or-unknown-reads-as-keep clause covers non-recycling and concurrent pools was wrong; that clause is acquisition-scoped. The controller-side default now lives in SPEC-5(b).

DECISION: SPEC-5(d) step-1 sidecar clause now points at "the runtime ends the **Lenny scrub procedure** paragraph names" — BECAUSE SPEC-5(c) is the one home of the restart-boundary order (end, sweep, cleanup, end, sweep) and step 1 named only the first end — ALTERNATIVES: naming both ends in step 1 restates the order and goes stale.
OPEN: summary.md bullets saying the runtime is ended, and agent-owned objects removed, "before `cleanupCommands`" omit the second end and sweep after the commands; left for a later round as a separate finding.

SPEC-2(a) §4.7.10 stays a pointer to §5.2 — BECAUSE §5.2 is where the WarmPoolController fixes the label, so the default belongs beside the rule that writes it, and D-PODLIFETIME and CODE-4 (non-spec-changes.md "otherwise `keep`, on every agent pod including embedded ones") already build exactly that — ALTERNATIVES: restoring "or a pool that does not recycle" in §4.7.10 (rejected: a second statement of the lifetime rule outside its home, and it would still miss concurrent and single-session or vm-restart recycling pools); leaning on "An acquisition reads an absent or unknown value of the label as `keep`" (rejected: that sentence governs the gateway's reading of a label, not what the controller writes or what the adapter and supervisor do on the pod).
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION at line ~647 "absent label reads as keep, which covers non-recycling and concurrent pools"]: the absent-label sentence is acquisition-scoped and assigns no lifetime to a pod; the controller-side default in SPEC-5(b) is what covers those pods.

EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5(b) "An acquisition reads an absent or unknown value of the label as `keep`."

### [redesign2.2026-10-22]

DECISION: "A `keep` pod" and "a `restart` pod" are defined once, in SPEC-5(b) §5.2 **Runtime-process lifetime (`recycle.runtimeProcess`).**, as pods whose lifetime, fixed by the WarmPoolController at creation from the pool's `recycle.runtimeProcess` (or `keep` on a pool without the field) and recorded in the immutable label `lenny.dev/runtime-process`, is `keep` or `restart`. The paragraph states that only the WarmPoolController at pod creation, pool admission, and the runtime-process acknowledgment read the pool field — BECAUSE five rounds re-keyed lifetime sentences one at a time from the pool's current value to the pod's fixed value, and each partial re-keying produced the next finding — ALTERNATIVES: keeping "under `keep`" with a global reading rule (rejected: the phrase reads as the pool's current value, which is what the findings kept filing).
DECISION: Every lifetime-dependent staged sentence now names a `keep` pod or a `restart` pod: the edge-case table rows (restart-pod idle exit, keep-pod idle exit, the edit row), SPEC-2(a) first sentence and the "no occupancy-zero boundary" replacement, SPEC-2(b) **SDK-warm.** (each `preConnect` pod is a `keep` pod) and **Liveness.**, SPEC-2(c) trade-off cell, SPEC-2(d) `Shutdown` row (both replacements; the first cites §5.2), SPEC-5(b) **Recycling and integration levels.** replacements, SPEC-5(c) scrub order and the **Lenny scrub procedure** replacements, SPEC-5(d) steps 1 and 1b, SPEC-5(e) **What the scrub reaches.**, SPEC-5(f), SPEC-5(g) (first sentence, the edit rule, the appended paragraph's per-pod sentences), SPEC-13's exit rule, the summary's What-changes bullets, D-LIFETIME, D-ACK's reuse clause, D-PODLIFETIME, D-CLEANUP, D-TRANSPORT, D-CRASH, D-SDKWARM, D-PROPERTIES, the Goals bullet, the 0079 and 0096 impact rows, the non-spec process model, CODE-3, and the Tier 1, Tier 4, and Tier 10 lists. **Runtime supervisor.**, **Launch.**, **End.**, **Sweep.**, and **Crash.** already said "on a `restart` pod" and now read against the definition. — EVIDENCE: grep of the staged files for `under `keep``, `under `restart``, `keep pool`, `restart pool`, `both lifetimes`, `created under` returns no lifetime-dependent hit.
DECISION: The pool field stays named at its permitted places only: the SPEC-5(a) YAML examples, the SPEC-5(b) home and its admission sentences, the SPEC-5(g) acknowledgment requirement ("a reusing recycling pool under `recycle.runtimeProcess: keep`", "a sequential pool under `recycle.runtimeProcess: restart`", "an admitted edit of `recycle.runtimeProcess`"), the edge-case row about that edit, SPEC-1's `restartable` citation of §5.2 (admission), SPEC-14(b)'s Phase 12c deliverable name, D-ACK's acknowledgment predicate, CODE-4's pod-creation input, and CODE-5's admission and acknowledgment predicates.
DECISION: The supervisor's per-pod self-test result reaches the adapter in a new first message `ready` on `CH-SUPERVISE`, carrying `properties` with `pass` or `fail` per enumerated property; the adapter logs each failed property and, on a `restart` pod, exits non-zero before READY when the non-dumpable, sender-identification, or kill-reach property failed; on a `keep` pod nothing gates; PID-namespace init immunity gates no pod. The supervisor always dials, and `--require-properties` is withdrawn. A missing, late, or malformed `ready`, a first message other than `ready`, or a second `ready` is a link failure before READY. Staged in SPEC-4 item 8 **Property self-test.** (home), the SPEC-7 card's **Messages.**, **Preconditions.**, **Timing.**, and **Degradation.** bullets and the register and §28.8 rows, SPEC-3(a), the edge-case table, D-PROPERTIES, D-CHANNEL, D-READY, D-PODLIFETIME's rejected-alternative sentence, CODE-2 (`ready`, the `selftest` mode for `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor`), CODE-3 `AwaitSupervisor`, CODE-4, CODE-6, and the Tier 1 and Tier 3 lists — BECAUSE D-LOGS forbids supervisor output on the runtime's stdout and stderr, so the re-baseline's "logged by the adapter" had no carrier, and an exit before dialing left a failed `restart` pod with no recorded reason; the adapter knows its lifetime from `--runtime-process`, so one gate in the trusted adapter replaces a flag on the untrusted-side binary. The supervisor still runs no author code when it sends `ready`, so the report is trustworthy — ALTERNATIVES: a `properties` member on the first `pong` (rejected: READY would wait a ping interval, and `pong` is periodic); keeping the supervisor-side `--require-properties` exit and adding `ready` only for logging (rejected: two gates, and the `restart` failure would still leave no log line); a supervisor log line on stderr (rejected by D-LOGS, because the runtime inherits and can forge it).
CORRECTS [spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1 UNVERIFIED "SPEC-4 says PID-namespace init immunity 'is reported' ... has no mechanism"]: closed; `ready` carries every property result, and the adapter logs failures.
DECISION: The checklist is corrected: S1 names SPEC-1 only (SPEC-10 no longer exists) and the §17.4 example; S4 names the `keep` pod and `restart` pod terms and the non-§5.2 sections SPEC-5 edits; S5 names SPEC-9(c) and (d) and the §5.1 `capabilities.preConnect` sentence. "Spec files touched" lists spec/13 under SPEC-5, and the summary's SPEC-5 deliverable line names §13.2.

### [spec.6.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Nothing in spec/ or pkg/ keeps a `type: mcp` pool from recycling. §5.1 says Lenny manages the pool for `type: mcp`, §4.6.2 sizes "mcp-type pools", poolstore.go has no runtime-type check, and pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:173 runs the scrub, cleanupCommands included, for every runtime kind. A recycling `type: mcp` sidecar pod therefore has a scrub and no supervisor. SPEC-5(c), (d) step 1, and (e) say "in the sidecar model the runtime supervisor runs cleanupCommands" and "the adapter container runs no author process", and both statements are false on that pod. Filed. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:28; spec/04_system-components.md:565; spec-changes.md:440, :448, :460

The Shutdown-row replacement in SPEC-2(d) and the first-sentence replacement in SPEC-5(g) restate that lifetime without the DemoteSDK exception. Filed as drift. ### [spec.8.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: A start during an in-flight launch is closed by widening the existing clause in SPEC-2(b) **Launch.** ("a session that starts while a process is live, or while a launch is in flight, uses that process") and the matching subject of SPEC-3(b) step 7's skip sentence. The non-spec launch mutex (non-spec-changes.md CODE `SupervisedRuntime` bullet) is then the implementation of a stated rule — BECAUSE the spec bullet otherwise reads as failing the second concurrent first start — ALTERNATIVES: naming the launch wait as a second pod-level serialization in §28.6 (rejected: the §28.6 and CH-BARRIER "no pod-level barrier lock" sentences are about the checkpoint barrier and the operation lock on gateway-to-pod and intra-pod channels; an adapter-internal launch wait is no channel constraint, so editing them would put adapter internals into the channel register).
WATCHOUT: "barrier lock" in spec/28 (lines ~391, ~1848, CH-BARRIER row ~1952) means the CheckpointBarrier lock, not any pod-level lock in general. Do not cite it as evidence about launch serialization. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1848
OPEN: Whether a launch whose `launch` request fails counts as "launched" for the **Launch.** permit (and so whether the next start may relaunch) is not stated. Pre-existing; a waiter on a failed in-flight launch finds no live process and fails its start under the existing sentence either way.

### [spec.8.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The WarmPoolController re-derives the SDK-warm path on every reconcile from the pool's current `spec.sdkWarmDisabled` (resolveSDKWarm), not from a creation-time record, so a pod's projected `sdk_connecting` path and its creation-time warm-up launch (SPEC-2 **SDK-warm.**) can disagree after a breaker flip. This is harmless: ConfigureWorkspace and DemoteSDK both handle a pod with no live process. Not a finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go:69-98, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:180-205
FACT: Agent pods already render `RestartPolicy: Never`, which the CH-SUPERVISE Exclusivity guard relies on. No spec section stated it before this proposal. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37
FACT: The WarmPoolController reads pool values from the PSC-mirrored `SandboxTemplate.spec.*`, not from Postgres. A `recycle.runtimeProcess` edit therefore reaches newly created pods only after the PSC mirror catches up. During that lag, pods created after the edit keep the old lifetime. The per-pod label makes this benign: a used `keep` pod is refused after one session. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:612
DECISION: no Kubernetes-idiom findings in the staged spec edits for round 8 — BECAUSE the lifetime label is set at creation by the pod's creator and kept immutable by an in-process webhook, every status write keeps its existing owner, the gateway hot path never waits on a controller, and CH-SUPERVISE is entirely inside the pod — ALTERNATIVES: I rejected filing three things. The "WPC creates no pod for a non-restartable runtime, with no condition" gap is observability hardening. The unnamed supervisor-volume match key is something the implementor can derive from the stage container's mounts. The mirror-lag imprecision in "applies to the pods created after it" is benign.

### [spec.8.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: every agent pod is a `keep` pod unless created under `restart`, so embedded pods and `type: mcp` sidecar pods are `keep` pods. A `keep`-pod sentence that is true only of supervisor pods (processes the runtime started persist, IPC objects persist) is false for embedded pods, where step 1 kills the runtime's children and step 1b purges the adapter-UID IPC the embedded runtime owns. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:499-500 (steps 1 and 1b), :507 (tree "in the sidecar model so do the processes it started")
WATCHOUT: Launch bullet allows a launch only when none has happened since creation or the last attested end, and a start that finds no live process otherwise fails. That leaves a launched-but-not-yet-connected window on concurrent pools. The non-spec launch mutex closes it in code, but the spec does not state it, and §28.6 says no pod-level barrier exists beyond the operation lock. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) **Launch.**; spec/28_communication-channels.md:1848-1849
FACT: the spec states nowhere that agent pods run with `restartPolicy: Never`. The only statement is in code at pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37, yet the CH-SUPERVISE Exclusivity guard cites it. This review did not file it, because the card itself now states it.
FACT: agent_pod_state (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:483-497) has no runtime-process column, so the Postgres fallback claim cannot read the pod lifetime label. Reading an absent label as `keep` fails closed, so this review did not file it.

### [spec.8.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The agent pod's `runtime` container has no preStop hook and no probes; only the `adapter` container carries the preStop drain hook. So at pod termination the supervisor receives the kubelet's SIGTERM at T0, while the adapter's preStop drain is still running. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:95 (adapter Lifecycle), runtime container block below it
FACT: The coordinator hold timeout does not make the adapter exit. `onHoldTimeout` closes the sidecar runtime connection through `CloseListener` and terminates the started sessions, and the pod stays. On a `restart` pod the staged Liveness bullet does not retire the pod for that close; the boundary `end` replaces the process instead, which is consistent with the §10.1 citation edit. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:189-246
FACT: The whole-pod scrub runs on `context.Background()` with no adapter-side deadline. A `CH-SUPERVISE` request sent during the scrub is bounded only by the gateway's missing-report timeout, which retires the pod. That is enough for recovery, so it is not a finding. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:43-50
WATCHOUT: Service mode has no checkpoint and no per-slot failure isolation (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:576). The staged service-mode "adapter exits non-zero" Liveness rule, which can fire during pod termination, therefore loses nothing the spec promises. Do not file it as a drain regression.
USEFUL [spec.6.review-mechanism.1]: the WATCHOUT that defers the intra-pod MCP nonce on a relaunched `restart` process to 0084 kept me from re-filing it.

### [spec.8.review-security.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the security lens — BECAUSE every lifetime-dependent bound traces to trusted sources: the `lenny.dev/runtime-process` label is set by the WarmPoolController at creation and added to every immutable-label enumeration (§4.6.1 host-schedulable paragraph, §13.2 note, §17.2 item 5, §16.5 row); the adapter's lifetime comes from a WPC-rendered flag; the `ready` self-test is sent before any author code exists; an absent or unknown label reads as `keep`, which fails closed (used pods refused when the config is `restart`) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the item-8 sentence "no author-controlled byte crosses the channel" against `exited`'s exit status (rejected: wording, the status is strictly decoded and decides nothing); filing the §10.1 hold-timeout retarget for `restart` pods (rejected: the boundary end removes the credential-holding process at the same point the old text retired the pod).
FACT: the `lenny-pod-security` webhook and the `pkg/podsecurity` validator are Phase 14 deliverables (spec/18_build-sequence.md §18.33), while SPEC-14(a) puts the supervisor stage container in Phase 2. The Phase 14 bullet already leaves out the Container identity rule, so leaving out the SPEC-6 mount rule as well is no phase-order defect — EVIDENCE: spec/18_build-sequence.md:652
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5(c) gives the scrub order but does not state that the second end and sweep on a `restart` pod run after a failed `cleanupCommands` under `onScrubFailure: warn`. Only CODE (`EndRuntimeAgain` "runs whether or not the cleanup step failed") says so. I judged it below the bar, because the first end has already removed every runtime-origin process. A later lens that reads the order as aborting on cleanup failure should check the non-spec text first — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5(c); non-spec-changes.md:110
USEFUL [Standing context: MCP nonce deferral to 0084]: saved re-deriving the restart-pod MCP nonce collision.

### [spec.10.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the reliability lens for round 10 — BECAUSE the round-9 hunks (restart-pod lifetime keyed to the whole-pod scrub, in-flight launch join, SPEC-4 `ready` wording, SPEC-5 pool-keyed sites, SPEC-8(e)) add no retry, replay, or recovery path; every CH-SUPERVISE failure ends in pod retirement or a non-zero adapter exit, with no retry loop that could stampede — ALTERNATIVES: filing D-PODEXIT (rejected: a recorded decision whose fix is outside the staged spec; see WATCHOUT); filing the Postgres fallback claim's absent-label read (rejected: it degrades but does not stall, and the fallback stamps no drain request, spec/04_system-components.md:440, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:559 "The Postgres-backed fallback claim reads pod state from its mirror and stamps no drain request")
WATCHOUT: D-PODEXIT (summary.md:48) says the forwarded SIGTERM means "the runtime sees the same signal at the same time as before this proposal". The timing is the same, but the effect is not. Before this proposal the runtime was PID 1 of its container, so a runtime with no SIGTERM handler survived the kubelet SIGTERM. Under the supervisor it is not namespace init, so the default disposition kills it at T0 while the adapter's preStop eviction checkpoint is still running (the preStop hook is on the adapter container only, pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:95). SPEC-2 **Crash.** then fails each bound session with runtime_crash during a drain. The adapter shutdown and eviction proposal owns this; do not file it against 0087's staged spec. — EVIDENCE: summary.md:48; spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) **Pod termination.** and **Crash.**
WATCHOUT: On a sequential `restart` pool, the Postgres fallback claim cannot read `lenny.dev/runtime-process` (agent_pod_state has no such column, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:483-497), so it treats every used pod as `keep` and refuses it. On such a pool the fallback can claim only never-used pods. This is fail-closed and stamps no drain. It reduces availability and loses no state. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5(b) "An acquisition reads an absent or unknown value of the label as keep"
USEFUL [spec.8.review-reliability.1]: its FACTs on the runtime container having no preStop hook and on the scrub having no adapter-side deadline (gateway missing-report timeout retires) settled two recovery questions without re-reading the adapter.

### [spec.10.review-security.1]
FACT: The restart-pod exemption from acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation rests only on trusted sources: the immutable pod label lenny.dev/runtime-process (set by the WarmPoolController at CREATE, read by the gateway through its existing `get` on Pods) and the adapter's --runtime-process flag rendered in the same pod spec. Absent or unknown label reads as `keep`, which is the refusing reading. No in-pod self-report decides admission. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:523 (gateway `get` on Pods); proposal spec-changes.md:404, non-spec-changes.md:118,134
FACT: A `restart` pod is reachable only for a sidecar `type: agent` runtime with `command` (restartable), so every `restart` pod runs the supervisor; embedded and `type: mcp` pods are always `keep`. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:68, :414-415
WATCHOUT: In nonce-only mode CH-SUPERVISE has no authentication beyond ordering, and abstract sockets are shared across every container in the pod netns, so a deployer-injected container could race the supervisor's dial. Checked: the real supervisor then exits after its dial window, which ends the runtime container, so the outcome is denial of service, not a forged attested end. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:334, :591-597, :626; spec/13_security-model.md:30
WATCHOUT: The allowCrossTenantReuse edit drops "restarting the runtime process" from the stated preconditions. Review log line 496 already records this as no control change, because the platform never resets the pin. 0096 must not read the remaining text as a sufficient condition on a `keep` pod. Not refiled.

DECISION: no single-source finding filed in round 10 — BECAUSE every repeat found (restart-pod end in SPEC-2(a) and SPEC-5(b); step 1 and **What the scrub reaches.** keep/restart outcomes; supervisor exit on close in **Pod termination.**, the card's **Degradation.**, and the §28.8 row; the §28.8 property-gate clause) is a one-clause summary citing its home — ALTERNATIVES: filing the **Launch.** "live or in flight" clause and step 7's skip subject as a (g) copy; rejected because review-log line 837 records the matching step 7 subject as deliberate and it is a clause.

### [spec.12.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: No reliability findings filed this round — BECAUSE every recovery path traced (supervisor death or freeze, link failure, crash with and without a bound session, warm-up failure, DemoteSDK end failure, keep-to-restart edit, hold timeout, pod termination) ends in a stated outcome with a reclaimer (Liveness -> ReportPodScrub "Runtime not live" retire, or "the next start fails and the gateway drains", spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:527) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the cleanup-request bound vs `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` race (rejected: the spec states no value for the request bound, so an implementor can set it above the worker's stop; this is a CODE-3 detail).
FACT: The adapter does not exit at the coordinator hold timeout; it closes the runtime's CH-MSGSOCK listener/connection and terminates sessions in two passes — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:159-232. The §10.1 sentence "Before exiting, the adapter SHOULD send..." reads as if it exits; it does not.
FACT: The gateway missing-report timeout is cleanupTimeoutSeconds + MissingReportGracePeriod (15s, operator-tunable); the adapter-side scrub has no deadline of its own — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go:24-37, pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:49. On a `restart` pod the two ends and two sweeps spend that grace.
FACT: Agent pods use restartPolicy Never in code (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37) but no spec section states it outside the staged CH-SUPERVISE Exclusivity bullet; SPEC-7(h) removes the two CrashLoopBackOff statements that contradicted it (spec/04_system-components.md:998, spec/10_gateway-internals.md:341).
WATCHOUT: On a `keep` pod, processes that `cleanupCommands` background now persist across the boundary (they run in the runtime container, and SPEC-5(d) keeps that container's processes on a `keep` pod). This follows from the settled D-CLEANUP; do not file it as a leak unless D-CLEANUP is reopened — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5(d); summary.md D-CLEANUP.

### [spec.12.review-security.1]
FACT: The trust inputs to the `restart`-pod reuse decision are all set by trusted components at pod creation: the label `lenny.dev/runtime-process` (WarmPoolController; NET-003 creation guard limits managed-pod CREATE to its ServiceAccount, and CODE-4 makes the label strictly immutable on UPDATE) and the adapter flag `--runtime-process` rendered in the same pod spec. ### [spec.16.fix-G1.1]
FACT: Setup commands (WorkspacePlan and runtime) run inside the adapter process at the adapter UID; the cancel path kills only the process group, so a setsid descendant survives into later sessions with write access to the credential tmpfs. Scrub steps 1 and 1b in the adapter container are the controls that end it, on every pod including supervised ones. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go Server.RunSetup -> workspace.RunSetup; pkg/adapter/workspace/setup.go Setpgid + Kill(-pid)
MISTAKE: Rounds before 16 staged a supervised-pod exemption for steps 1 and 1b in SPEC-5(d) ("the adapter container runs no author process") and a CODE-3 no-op of DefaultOps.KillUserProcesses/PurgeIPCShm under --supervise-socket. Both withdrawn: SPEC-5(d) now edits step 3 only; SPEC-5(e) reads "Steps 0 to 6 execute in the adapter's process"; CODE-3 implements both natively (kill(-1) and IPC_RMID by adapter UID) on every pod.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce any supervised-pod exemption for step 1 or 1b, nor a no-op under --supervise-socket. Do not add setup-command text to SPEC-5(g): its "no runtime process serves two sessions" holds because step 1 kills setup leftovers. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md step 1 sentence "The kill runs in the container that executes the scrub"
FACT: The implementation checklist names neither step 1b, the DefaultOps no-op, nor KillUserProcesses, so this fix leaves it true. — EVIDENCE: grep of the checklist

### [spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: restore scrub steps 1 and 1b in the adapter container on every pod by DELETING SPEC-5(d)'s step-1 replacement and step-1b append (the tree's step 1 sentence "The kill runs in the container that executes the scrub and does not end the runtime process" stays true on supervised pods), widening SPEC-5(e) "Steps 0 and 2 to 6" to "Steps 0 to 6", and making CODE-3's DefaultOps change native-and-unconditional instead of a no-op under --supervise-socket — BECAUSE setup commands (WorkspacePlan and runtime) run in the adapter container at the adapter UID with the credential tmpfs mounted read-write, so a setsid/daemon leftover survives the command's process-group kill and only step 1/1b end it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's longer step-1 replacement (adds a sentence (e) already states); keeping exec-based DefaultOps on unsupervised pods (dual path, and exec fails on the distroless image anyway).
FACT: setup commands run in-process in the adapter container: Server.RunSetup -> workspace.RunSetup; the child gets Setpgid and Cancel kills -pgid only, so a setsid descendant escapes — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:394, pkg/adapter/workspace/setup.go:205,215; adapter mounts the credential volume without ReadOnly — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:25-35
FACT: DefaultOps.SandboxUser is never set (cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:111 leaves it empty), so the su path is dead; a native kill(-1, SIGKILL) from the adapter process reaches every adapter-container process it can signal except itself and init — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/defaultops.go:32-45
WATCHOUT: "the adapter container runs no author process" is false whenever setup commands exist; do not reintroduce a supervised-pod exemption for step 1 or 1b. Agent-UID IPC on a keep pod persists by design and needs no step-1b sentence; (e) and **Sweep.** carry the restart-pod case.
UNVERIFIED: whether a tier-5 setup command can exec a probe binary in the distroless adapter container (workspace delivery plus mount exec flags); the TEST-1 author should confirm before writing the case.

### [spec.16.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The tree's sidecar liveness predicate is "connected and not ended": `ServesNextSession` returns `p.connected && !p.ended`, so a runtime that never dialled is reported not live. `Start` bounds the accept by `AcceptTimeout` (default 30s) and leaves the accept loop running, so a late dial is installed for the next Start. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:222-250, :455-459
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2(d) replaces the ReportPodScrub "a sidecar runtime process whose connection is open is reported as able to" criterion with a citation of **Liveness.**, whose list has no never-connected case. On a `keep` pod, a step-7-launched process that never dials is then reported live, and **Launch.** makes the next session reuse it. Only the warm-up launch has a bounded connect wait in **Launch.**. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) Liveness and Launch bullets; spec/04_system-components.md:695
FACT: The SDK-warm circuit breaker counts demotions only, so a warm-up launch that fails every time never trips it. This predates 0087: `sdk_connecting -> failed` already behaved this way, so it is not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:48, :90

### [spec.16.review-security.1]
FACT: Session setup commands (the client's WorkspacePlan setupCommands and the runtime's) run per session in the adapter process, at the adapter UID in the adapter container, which mounts the credential volume read-write. RunSetup kills only the command's process group, so a descendant that calls setsid outlives the command. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:394-450; pkg/adapter/workspace/setup.go (Setpgid plus kill of -pgid); pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:25-35; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:378
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5(d) says "the adapter container runs no author process" and drops scrub steps 1 and 1b on supervised pods, and summary.md:64 gives the same reason. Both are false while setup commands run in the adapter container. Any remedy has to keep an adapter-container kill and the adapter-UID IPC purge on every pod. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:471, :474
USEFUL [spec.12.review-edit-sites.1 FACT "setup commands run in the adapter process"]: this entry pointed me to the setup-command path that SPEC-5(d)'s step-1 claim overlooks.
FACT: The lenny-label-immutability decision compares oldV != newV, so an UPDATE that adds lenny.dev/runtime-process to a pod that lacked it is refused. An attacker cannot turn an unlabeled keep pod into a restart pod by adding the label. EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:132-145

### [spec-5d.fail-closed.2026-10-22]
FACT: SPEC-5(d)'s premise "the adapter container runs no author process" is false, and the current staging no longer relies on it. Session setup commands, the client's WorkspacePlan commands and the runtime's `setupCommands`, run per session in the adapter process at the adapter UID on every pod, supervised or not, and their cancel path kills only the command's process group, so a `setsid` descendant survives the command. `cleanupCommands` leave the adapter container only on supervised pods (D-CLEANUP); on embedded and `type: mcp` sidecar pods they still run there. `MCPRuntime` is not wired in `cmd/lenny-adapter`, and `SocketRuntimeProcess.spawn` is the developer loop only. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go `Server.RunSetup` -> `workspace.RunSetup`; pkg/adapter/workspace/setup.go (`Setpgid`, `cmd.Cancel` kills `-pid`); pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindprepare.go `cl.RunSetup`; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go `spawn`; pkg/adapter/mcpruntime.go
DECISION: Fail closed: steps 1 and 1b keep running in the adapter container on every pod. Round 16 (`[spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]`) had already deleted the step-1 replacement and step-1b append from SPEC-5(d), and CODE-3 already runs `DefaultOps.KillUserProcesses` and `PurgeIPCShm` natively on every pod. This pass tightened SPEC-5(d)'s find string to the exact step-3 line (`3. Purge environment variables injected for the previous session (tracked by the adapter; restored to the pod baseline set recorded at first boot).`, one occurrence in spec/05) and added to SPEC-5(e) the sentence "Steps 1 and 1b run on every pod, including a pod that runs the runtime supervisor, because a process started in the adapter container during a session can outlive that session." — BECAUSE the (e) paragraph describes what the runtime end reaches on a `restart` pod, and without an explicit statement a reader could take the runtime end as a replacement for step 1 — ALTERNATIVES: naming setup commands in the sentence, rejected because the specification does not state which container runs setup commands, so the sentence states the behavior without that claim.
FACT: The SPEC-5(e) find strings still resolve once each in spec/05 (`**What the scrub reaches.** The scrub executes in the adapter's process and reaches what that process can reach.` and its closing sentence). The summary's watch-out "`scrub.DefaultOps` execs `sh` and `ipcrm`" states a tree fact and gives no exemption reason, so it stays.

### [spec.17.review-security.1]
FACT: The §5.2 scrub code runs steps 1-6 even after a failed cleanupCommands run, so the staged second end and sweep after cleanupCommands also run when cleanup fails or times out. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go:255-262 ("Steps 1-6 run regardless of a cleanup failure")
FACT: §4.7.9 step 3 ("Adapter writes placeholder manifest") comes before step 4, and the staged step-4 append puts the supervisor's connection and `ready` inside step 4. So a manifest exists before the supervisor connects in the numbered order, which contradicts the staged CH-SUPERVISE card clause "because the supervisor connects before any manifest exists". Filed. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:909; spec-changes.md:614-616
DECISION: Did not file the sweep's walk bound ("bounds its walk") as an unmarked fail-closed blank — BECAUSE the workspace base outside slots/ is never cleared or verified on any pod today, and step-2 or step-6 failures under `onScrubFailure: warn` already leave residual files on reused pods, so a bound-hit that leaves files is no regression of an established control. Residual files are covered by acknowledgeBestEffortScrub — ALTERNATIVES: requiring that a bound-hit fail the sweep (this would harden, but the change is optional).
WATCHOUT: CODE-2 (non-spec-changes.md:93) says "bounded depth and entry counts" and does not say whether reaching the bound fails the sweep. The code loop should make a bound-hit return an `error` on `swept`, which fails closed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:93
FACT: The §28.6 sole-session MCP attribution rule ("refuses the call unless that process has been given exactly one session") becomes per-generation under SPEC-2 **Generation.** The adapter, a trusted component, is the counter's source, and an attested end precedes every new generation, so the reset relaxes nothing. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1871-1875

FACT: `exited` cannot arrive with a stale `gen` in a correct supervisor: `launch` (which advances `gen`) is sent only after an attested `ended`, and an `exited` for the old generation precedes `ended` on the ordered stream. A stale-gen `exited` therefore signals a supervisor defect, which the card's link-failure rule correctly retires. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) **Launch.** and SPEC-7(d) **Degradation.**

### [spec.21.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Sweep.** now classifies a walk that reaches its bound as a failed sweep — BECAUSE whether residue blocks reuse is a fail-closed predicate, and a restart pod is ack-exempt, so a truncated sweep would hand agent-written content in `/sessions`, `/artifacts`, or the workspace base outside `slots/` to the next process with no warning and no retirement — ALTERNATIVES: IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE (barred for a fail-closed predicate); stating the outcome in CODE-2 (the normative home is the spec bullet); leaving it open (two verifiers confirmed the gap).
CORRECTS [spec.17.review-security.1]: its DECISION not to file the walk bound as a fail-closed blank is superseded; the bound-hit outcome is now stated in SPEC-2(b) **Sweep.** and its consequence (retirement whatever `onScrubFailure` states) comes from **Liveness.** and SPEC-5(c). Its WATCHOUT on CODE-2 "bounded depth and entry counts" stays true and matches the fix.
WATCHOUT: do not add a bound value, a new error code, or a `scrub_warning` path for a bound-hit; the existing failed-sweep path covers it. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) **Sweep.** bullet.

### [spec.21.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: At pod termination the supervisor sends no signal on the stop request and exits when CH-SUPERVISE closes (SPEC-2 **Pod termination.**, edge row, D-PODEXIT, impacts row, CODE-2, TEST-1 Tier 8) — BECAUSE the runtime is no longer PID 1, the kubelet signals only PID 1, the runtime container has no preStop, and a forwarded SIGTERM kills an SDK runtime (no handler) at T0, so **Crash.** reports runtime_crash during the §4.6.1 preStop eviction checkpoint — ALTERNATIVES: drop the stop-request filter entirely (plan §10.3 requires si_pid/si_code filter and the sender-identification gate); new adapter stop message on CH-SUPERVISE (new protocol surface, belongs to the adapter shutdown proposal/R16); exempt post-stop exits from **Crash.** (runtime still dead before checkpoint_request).
CORRECTS [review-log line 62 DECISION "the supervisor forwards the kubelet's SIGTERM at once (D-PODEXIT)"]: superseded; the supervisor sends no signal at pod termination.
WATCHOUT: Do not "restore" a tini-style SIGTERM forward in the supervisor; the TEST-1 Tier 8 case "deleting a supervised pod sends its runtime process no signal before the adapter exits" is the discriminating assertion — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go runtime container has no Lifecycle, adapter carries preStopDrainHook; spec/04_system-components.md **Disruption protection for agent pods**; spec/07 §7.3 pod_evicted vs runtime_crash checkpoint statement.
FACT: sdks/runtime/python and sdks/runtime/typescript register no SIGTERM handler — EVIDENCE: grep SIGTERM over both returns nothing.
OPEN: Whether the stop-request filter still earns its place once it triggers no action (it now only distinguishes "ignore" from "ignore"); its removal would touch plan §10.3's sender-identification property, an owner record. Not reopened this round.

### [spec.21.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Sweep.** "and bounds its walk." becomes "and bounds its walk; a walk that reaches its bound is a failed sweep." BECAUSE it is a fail-closed predicate and the existing "A failed sweep retires the pod" outcome (**Liveness.**, SPEC-5(c)) already carries the consequence, so no new mechanism is needed. ALTERNATIVES: leave open as implementor's choice (barred for a fail-closed predicate); state an outcome in CODE-2 (the rule's home is the spec bullet).
CORRECTS [spec.17.review-security.1]: the DECISION not to file the walk bound is superseded; round 21 staged the bound-hit-fails rule in SPEC-2(b). The WATCHOUT beside it (code makes a bound-hit return `error` on `swept`) is now the spec's rule and stays consistent.
WATCHOUT: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 `sweep` bullet "bounded depth and entry counts" needs no edit; its outcome follows from SPEC-2(b). Do not restate the outcome there. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:93

### [spec.21.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: At pod termination the supervisor sends no signal on the stop request; SPEC-2 **Pod termination.** is replaced in place, D-PODEXIT becomes the reason home, and the edge row, CODE-2 signal-handling line, and the shutdown-proposal impacts row are cut to match — BECAUSE the runtime is no longer its container's PID 1, so a forwarded SIGTERM kills an SDK runtime with no handler at T0 and **Crash.** turns every drain into runtime_crash during the adapter's preStop eviction checkpoint — ALTERNATIVES: delete the stop request and the sender-identification property (rejected: plan §10.3 trust model gates restart pods on four properties, sender identification among them, an owner record); a new CH-SUPERVISE stop message from the adapter (rejected: the adapter already holds `end`, and the shutdown proposal owns drain ordering); exempting post-stop exits from **Crash.** (rejected: hair, and the runtime would still be dead before the checkpoint).
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION "At pod termination the supervisor forwards the kubelet's SIGTERM at once (D-PODEXIT)"]: superseded; the supervisor sends no signal at pod termination.
CORRECTS [spec.10.review-reliability.1]: its WATCHOUT deferred the T0 kill to the shutdown proposal; it is a defect in 0087's own staged text, because the forward was 0087's mechanism, and it is closed here.
FACT: The kubelet signals only a container's PID 1, and the runtime container has no preStop hook, so with no forward the runtime lives until the adapter exits (closing CH-SUPERVISE, whose close makes the supervisor exit and the kernel kill the rest) or the grace-period SIGKILL. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go (runtime container has no Lifecycle; adapter has preStopDrainHook); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Disruption protection for agent pods**; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md **External side effects across recovery** (pod_evicted vs runtime_crash checkpoints).
WATCHOUT: After this fix the stop request has no action; the `si_pid`/`si_code` filter and the sender-identification property survive only because plan §10.3 (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md, gVisor spike and trust model of 2026-10-02) requires them. Do not file the dormant filter as hair and do not delete the property without the owner. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md "acting on a shutdown signal only when `si_pid == 0`" and "gated on the same four properties".
OPEN: Whether the stop request should carry any action (it now has none) belongs to the adapter shutdown and eviction proposal; if it gives none, the owner may retire the filter and the sender-identification gate together.

### [spec.21.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Filed one finding: SPEC-2(b) **Pod termination.** forwards the kubelet SIGTERM at T0, and **Crash.** then fails each bound session with `runtime_crash` during a graceful drain, which defeats the agent-pod preStop eviction checkpoint (spec/04_system-components.md:509) and the `pod_evicted` classification (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:449) — BECAUSE the forward is staged spec text, so the remedy lands in the staged spec, and the round-10 rejection ("fix is outside the staged spec") was mistaken — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it to the unwritten adapter shutdown and eviction proposal (rejected: the applied spec would state a crash classification for every graceful drain in the meantime, and no settled-decision list names D-PODEXIT).
CORRECTS [spec.10.review-reliability.1]: its DECISION that D-PODEXIT's fix is "outside the staged spec" is wrong; SPEC-2(b) **Pod termination.** carries the forward and the edge-case row at spec-changes.md:45 restates it. Its WATCHOUT on the PID-1 effect is right and is the basis of this round's finding.
FACT: The Python and TypeScript runtime SDKs install no SIGTERM handler (grep of sdks/runtime/python/lenny_runtime and sdks/runtime/typescript/src finds none), so a runtime built on them is immune to the kubelet SIGTERM as PID 1 today and survives until the grace-period SIGKILL. Go runtimes die on SIGTERM even as PID 1, because the Go runtime installs handlers. EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/python/lenny_runtime/lifecycle.py, sdks/runtime/typescript/src/lifecycle.ts, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:475
FACT: Full-level checkpoints need the live runtime (`checkpoint_request`/`checkpoint_ready` on CH-RUNTIMEOPS), so a runtime ended at T0 cannot give the eviction checkpoint a consistent snapshot. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:257
DEFERRED [summary.md]: D-PODEXIT says the forward means "the runtime sees the same signal at the same time as before this proposal". The timing is the same, but the effect differs for a handlerless runtime, which was PID-1-immune before and is killed under the supervisor. Whatever the fixer chooses, D-PODEXIT and the adapter shutdown proposal impacts row (summary.md:110) must follow it.
USEFUL [spec.16.review-reliability.1]: the FACT that a never-connected keep-pod runtime is reported not live through `connected && !ended` let me skip re-tracing the step-7 never-dials case.

### [redesign3.2026-10-22]
DECISION: The owner kept the `restart` lifetime in part 1 after the review run stopped (stopped-redesign, spec round 21). The two mechanisms the stop named were converged as one table each, derived from the tree and the staging, and the citing sites were regenerated against them.
DECISION: SPEC-5(c) gains the §5.2 **Supervised scrub sequence.** table, inserted before **What the scrub reaches.**, as the one home of each step's outcome on a supervised pod — BECAUSE the outcomes were stated piecemeal in SPEC-2 **Sweep.**, the SPEC-5(c) order sentence, the card's **Degradation.**, the edge-case table, and CODE-2/CODE-3, and two of them had drifted (CODE-2's sweep bound had no outcome; the second end after a failed cleanup was stated only in CODE-3) — ALTERNATIVES: one sentence per step in the order paragraph (rejected: a mid-paragraph insertion and no place for the exit-race column).
DECISION: Every row through steps 1 to 6 runs whatever an earlier row's outcome was, except a row a link failure skips — BECAUSE `scrub.Run` already runs steps 1 to 6 after a cleanup failure (pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go, "Steps 1-6 run regardless of a cleanup failure"), every failed supervisor step already retires the pod, and continuing cleans as much as possible before the retirement — ALTERNATIVES: stopping at the first not-live outcome (rejected: skips the deployer's `cleanupCommands`, which may do external cleanup, and gains nothing because the pod retires).
DECISION: The second end and second sweep run when `cleanupCommands` fail or outlast `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` (owner-delegated), stated in the `cleanupCommands` row's error cell and D-CLEANUP.
DECISION: A sweep walk that reaches its bound is not live (the table's bound column), replacing the clause round 21 put in SPEC-2 **Sweep.**; CODE-2 now makes a bound-hit reply `swept` with an error code and cites the table.
DECISION: A link failure during the sequence makes the adapter report not live and send no further request, so every later row that sends one is skipped — BECAUSE requests on a link the adapter has declared failed have no defined reply, and the pod retires anyway — ALTERNATIVES: closing `CH-SUPERVISE` on a link failure (rejected: new behavior beyond the stop's scope, and it would fail held sessions mid-session on a `keep` pod).
DECISION: The listener rebind gets a row, and its failure is not live; SPEC-2 **Liveness.** lists a failed rebind — BECAUSE the rebind had no stated failure outcome, and a listener that is not rebound can hand the next generation a stale connection — ALTERNATIVES: none considered.
DECISION: The launch of the next generation runs only when **Launch.** permits it and no row reported not live; CODE-3's launch check refuses while `notLive` is set — BECAUSE the launch permit is set by any attested end, so a first end that succeeded followed by a second end that failed would otherwise permit a launch on a pod about to retire — ALTERNATIVES: relying on the gateway's retirement alone (rejected: fail closed in the adapter).
DECISION: A launched process that exits before it holds its first session is not live (SPEC-2 **Liveness.**, launch row) — BECAUSE on a `restart` pod no other condition catches it, and the pod would be reused with a runtime that dies at start — ALTERNATIVES: failing only the start (rejected: fails open on repeat).
DECISION: The round-21 mechanism lens's UNVERIFIED race (an `exited` after the last `session_end` against the slot release) is closed by definition. SPEC-2 **Crash.** defines "holds a session" as the span from the §4.7.1 rule-8 record to the deregistration of the session's registry entry, and the adapter handles `exited` under the registry critical section. The release row of the table states that the `Shutdown` deregisters before it writes `session_end`, so an exit answering that `session_end` is no crash, and one handled before the deregistration is a crash that retires the pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go `Shutdown` (`reclaimSlotLocked` under `s.mu`) and `tearDownReclaimedSlot` (`writeSessionEnd` after the deregistration); spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.** and rule 8.
DECISION: SPEC-2(b) **Signals.** becomes the one home of the signal model, a table of signal sources and the supervisor's action; **Pod termination.** merges into its kubelet row — BECAUSE the signal actions were split across **Signals.**, **Pod termination.**, D-SIGNALS, D-PODEXIT, and CODE-2, and D-SIGNALS still carried the pre-round-21 STOPSIGNAL rationale — ALTERNATIVES: a separate §4.7.11 home (rejected: the signals are the supervisor's mechanism, and §4.7.11 item 8 is the trust basis).
DECISION: The supervisor acts on no catchable signal other than SIGCHLD, SIGPIPE, and its own fault signals, and the `si_pid`/`si_code` stop-request filter is withdrawn — BECAUSE after round 21 the stop request had no action, so the filter selected between two no-ops; plan §10.3's "acting on a shutdown signal only when `si_pid == 0`" holds because the supervisor acts on no shutdown signal — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the filter to log the kubelet's signal (rejected: D-LOGS keeps the supervisor off stdout and stderr, and `CH-SUPERVISE` has no message for it); keeping it to exit when `CH-SUPERVISE` closes (rejected: the close itself makes the supervisor exit, with no signal involved).
DECISION: Sender identification stays in the self-test and the `restart` gating set (owner record), SPEC-4 item 8 says plainly that no supervisor action depends on it, and the summary records a non-blocking owner question on retiring it.
DECISION: The SPEC-7 card's **Messages.** bullet states that the specification does not name the members of `cleanup` and `exited`, the property names, or the error codes, and that the two binaries fix them together; CODE-2's IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE lists them and the TEST-1 tier-3 contract test pins them (owner-delegated). This closes the finding spec.21.review-applicability.1 filed.
DECISION: TEST-1 gains a tier-1 case per cell of the scrub sequence table, a tier-1 C-harness case per row of the signal table, tier-3 pinning of the unnamed members, a tier-4 bound-hit retirement, a tier-7a race of `exited` against the last session's deregistration, a tier-8 case with a catchable image `STOPSIGNAL`, and a tier-9 case for catchable signals to PID 1. The tier-8 case that SIGKILL and SIGSTOP from the probe retire the pod was wrong on runc, where the namespace init is immune; it now asserts the supervisor keeps answering `ping` there, and the retirement case moves to tier 6.
CORRECTS [spec.21.fix-design-G2.1 WATCHOUT "Do not file the dormant filter as hair and do not delete the property without the owner"]: the filter is withdrawn because the signal table gives it no job; the property is kept, as the owner recorded, with an owner question.
CORRECTS [spec.21.fix-G1.1 DECISION placing the bound-hit outcome in SPEC-2 **Sweep.**]: the outcome now lives in the scrub sequence table, and **Sweep.** cites it.
CORRECTS [spec.21.fix-design-G1.1 WATCHOUT "CODE-2 ... needs no edit"]: CODE-2 now states that a bound-hit replies `swept` with an error code and cites the table, as the owner's stop note required.
FACT: The gateway's missing-report timeout (`cleanupTimeoutSeconds` plus a grace period, §5.2 **Lenny scrub procedure.**) bounds the whole sequence, which now includes two ends, two sweeps, and the rebind; a sequence that outlasts it retires the pod. `startPodScrub` runs the scrub under `context.Background()`, so CODE-3 now requires a deadline on the scrub's supervisor requests. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/podscrub.go `startPodScrub`.
WATCHOUT: If the grace period is shorter than two ends and two sweeps on a loaded node, `restart` pods retire through the missing-report timeout. The specification states no grace value; the code loop should size the request deadlines against it.


### [spec.22.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: a step-0 error skips the `cleanupCommands` row, and the SPEC-5(c) Step 0 row's Error cell is the one home of that rule; the paragraph says only "other than a row an earlier row's outcome skips" — BECAUSE §5.2 step 0 (unedited) says every credential file MUST be purged before any deployer-defined code runs, and the cleanup worker runs at the agent UID in a container that mounts the credential volume — ALTERNATIVES: listing the exception in the paragraph (second statement of the cell); retiring or marking not live on a step-0 error (unneeded, step 6 re-checks and onScrubFailure decides the pod); skipping every later row (steps 1 to 6 are still scrub work).
FACT: `scrub.Run` runs `runCleanup` after a step-0 `removeAll` error with no gate, on every pod — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/scrub.go:247-258; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:495 states the step-0 MUST.
WATCHOUT: the gate belongs in `scrub.Run` for every pod; a gate only on the `CleanupRunner` path leaves the unsupervised-pod gap — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md `scrub.Run` bullet now says "which `scrub.Run` applies on every pod".
CORRECTS [Standing context, Supervised scrub sequence home, "Every row through steps 1 to 6 runs whatever an earlier row's outcome was, except a row a link failure skips"]: a step-0 error also skips the `cleanupCommands` row (Step 0 row, Error cell). Same for review-log DECISION at line ~1643.

### [spec.22.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Delegated decision (A) applied in the SPEC-2(b) **Signals.** table: the Any-sender row excludes SIGCHLD, SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGFPE, and SIGILL, and the fault row's source is "Any sender, and the kernel on a fault in the supervisor" — BECAUSE a kernel-only fault row needs si_code/si_pid to tell a real fault from a sent one, which falsifies the lead-in and SPEC-4 item 8 and can spin a keep-pod supervisor whose self-test failed — ALTERNATIVES: si_code classification (sender identification, rejected by (A)); carving fault signals out of the lead-in or SPEC-4 (unneeded once the table is fixed).
WATCHOUT: The edge-case row "A runtime process sends the supervisor a catchable signal" is now a pure pointer ("As the signal table states"); do not re-add a "No action" or fault-signal clause there, which restates the table — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md edge-case table.
FACT: A forged SIGPIPE falls under the Any-sender row (no action); only the kernel's SIGPIPE row has the closed-connection outcome, so SIGPIPE stays out of the Any-sender exclusion list — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Signals table SIGPIPE row.
FACT: CODE-2's signal bullet already `_exit`s on fault signals from any sender, so it needed no change — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 "Signal handling implements each row".

### [spec.22.fix-design-G1.1]

### [spec.23.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-5 scrub table `cleanupCommands` row Error cell stays the one home of the cleanup failure conditions and now names a command ended by a signal and a worker that exits abnormally. The edge-case index row is cut to a pointer ("The supervisor's `cleaned` reply carries an error code"), and CODE-2 cites the row — BECAUSE the index row and CODE-2 each re-listed the cell's conditions and both went stale (fail-open) when the cell was incomplete — ALTERNATIVES: widening the index row too (a second copy that drifts); moving conditions into **Cleanup.** (inverts the established home).
DECISION: The owner's accepted keep-pod residue (runtime at the same UID can stop, signal, read, or trace the cleanup worker and commands; confined to its own tenant-pinned pod; a disrupted cleanup fails the scrub) has one home, SPEC-2 **Cleanup.**'s last sentence. Do not add it to the summary or an index row.
WATCHOUT: A signalled child's `WEXITSTATUS` reads 0, so "exits non-zero" alone classifies a SIGKILLed cleanup command or worker as success on a `keep` pod. CODE-2 `cleanup` names this trap once. The worker's own deadline SIGKILL is `cleanup_timeout`, never `cleanup_failed` — EVIDENCE: proposal non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 `cleanup` bullet; spec-changes.md SPEC-5(c) `cleanupCommands` row.
FACT: TEST-1 Tier 1 C-harness clause now pins a SIGKILLed cleanup command and a SIGKILLed cleanup worker replying `cleaned` with `cleanup_failed`. The clause sits inside the sweep-walker parenthetical, which a later round may wish to move out to read cleanly.

### [spec.23.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the SPEC-5 scrub table `cleanupCommands` row Error cell stays the one home for which cleanup results fail the scrub; it gains the signalled command, the abnormal worker exit, and the failed exec; the failure-mode index row (spec-changes "Edge cases and accepted failure modes") is cut to a pointer ("`cleaned` carries an error code") rather than re-listing conditions — BECAUSE the index row re-listed the cell's conditions and went stale the moment the cell grew — ALTERNATIVES: extending the index row's condition list too (a second statement that drifts again).
DECISION: the keep-pod residue (live runtime at the agent UID can stop, kill, read, or ptrace the cleanup worker and commands) is one sentence at SPEC-2 **Cleanup.**, per owner delegated decision (2) of 2026-10-22; no separate index row — BECAUSE a second row would restate it.
WATCHOUT: a child ended by a signal has WIFSIGNALED true and WEXITSTATUS 0; a cleanup worker that tests only WEXITSTATUS reports success. Stated once, in CODE-2's `cleanup` bullet. A SIGKILL the worker itself sends at the deadline is `cleanup_timeout`, not `cleanup_failed` — the deadline takes precedence. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:96
WATCHOUT: on a `keep` pod the first end and sweep rows do not run (spec-changes.md:485 lead-in), so the runtime is live during `cleanupCommands`; that is why this fail-open mattered only on `keep` pods.
OPEN: a worker status lost to the generic wait4(-1) reaper (security-review fix 4) is also a failure; this design does not word it into the Error cell — the group owning fix (4) should confirm whether the cell or CODE-2 is its home.

### [spec.23.follow-up-fix.1]
CORRECTS [spec.23.fix-G1 DECISION; spec.23.fix-design-G1 DECISION]: the keep-pod residue is "stop or signal the worker; stop, signal, read, or trace the commands". The cleanup worker is a fork of the supervisor without execve, so it inherits PR_SET_DUMPABLE 0 and same-UID code cannot read or ptrace it; only the execve'd commands become dumpable. SPEC-2 **Cleanup.** last sentence now says so. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 **Protection from author code.** ("marks itself non-dumpable"); non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 `run` (prctl before serving) and `cleanup` (fork, then execve per command).
WATCHOUT: any future restatement of the keep-pod residue must keep the worker/commands split; fork preserves non-dumpable, execve resets it.
