### [f8.human-decisions]
DECISION: Rewrote the summary's open decision "Retire the sender-identification property (non-blocking)" (summary.md `## Open decisions for human to make`, leading text kept verbatim, no identifier added) so it states the question in reader terms, recommends keep as staged (SPEC-4 item 8, spec-changes.md:410-420; CODE-3, non-spec-changes.md:110) on the owner record gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3175-3178 and the runc/gVisor result :3163-3165, gives retire as the alternative and why it lost, the cost of each answer, and confidence moderate. The earlier text recommended retire, against the staged deliverable. No staged change file edited; no checklist change.
OPEN: The round-23 entries (`[spec.23.review-security.1]`) mention a delegated "sender-identification retirement" directive of 2026-10-22 that no record in the tree, git history, or memory holds. If a later firing finds it is a real delegated decision, this item becomes resolve: retire.

### [redesign4.2026-10-22]
DECISION: Owner decision of 2026-10-22, after a security review found the design equivalent or better: sender identification is retired from the self-test, the `restart` gating set, `lenny-ctl preflight supervisor`, SPEC-4 item 8, CODE-2, CODE-3, CODE-6, and TEST-1, and the summary's open decision about it is removed — BECAUSE no supervisor action reads a signal's sender — ALTERNATIVES: keep it as a gate with no job (the `[f8.human-decisions]` recommendation, overruled by the owner). SPEC-13's row names no property, so it needed no edit.
DECISION: D-BINARY's exclusion of a Go supervisor no longer rests on `si_pid`; it rests on the Go runtime's own fault-signal handlers, which conflict with keeping those signals at `SIG_DFL` and blocked, and on its threads, which make the workers' fork without `execve` unsafe.
DECISION: Fault signals (under the owner's delegation, replacing delegated decision (A)): no handler; `SIG_DFL` and blocked in the supervisor; every child sets an empty signal mask before `execve`. The Signals table splits the fault signals into a sent row (no effect) and a kernel synchronous-fault row (the supervisor ends). This restores plan §10.3's "only `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` remain effective" on runc — BECAUSE the exit-on-fault handler made four more signals effective for any same-UID sender (`[spec.23.review-security.1]` WATCHOUT) — ALTERNATIVES: the exit-on-fault handler (rejected for that reason); an `si_code` test to tell a real fault from a sent one (sender identification, retired).
FACT: On Linux a signal that is blocked is queued as pending rather than discarded (`sig_ignored` in `kernel/signal.c` returns false for a blocked signal), so a sent fault signal to the supervisor most likely stays pending on runc as well as on gVisor; the directive said "dropped (runc)". The Signals row says "stays pending or is discarded", which is true either way, and fork clears a child's pending set, so a pending forged fault signal never reaches a child. A synchronous fault is delivered through the block (`force_sig_info_to_task` unblocks it and resets the action to the default, and clears the init's unkillable flag for it). UNVERIFIED on gVisor; the tier-6 forced-delivery case checks it.
DECISION: Implementation hazards (under the owner's delegation) are CODE-2 requirements: the SIGCHLD flag is cleared before the reap loop; every supervisor and worker descriptor is close-on-exec; every syscall is retried on `EINTR`; every signal from 1 to `_NSIG - 1` other than SIGKILL, SIGSTOP, and the fault signals has a flag-only handler, real-time signals included; no `alarm` or `setitimer` (deadlines from `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` with `ppoll`); the kill-reach self-test passes only when the grandchild's `/proc` entry is present before the `kill(-1)` and absent after. TEST-1's C harness has a case for each.
DECISION: Accepted bound (under the owner's delegation): a process that a cleanup command moves out of its process group escapes the deadline SIGKILL; on a `restart` pod the second end ends it, and on a `keep` pod it persists as the runtime does. Stated once, at the end of SPEC-2 **Cleanup.**'s deadline sentence.
FACT: The worker can only send the deadline SIGKILL to a process group (`kill(-pgid)`); no syscall signals a session. The staging said "the running command's session". **Cleanup.**, the Signals table's deadline row, CODE-2, and the Standing context now say process group, noting that the worker starts each command as a new session, and the bound covers `setpgid` as well as `setsid`.
DECISION: **Supervisor child-process model.** A new SPEC-2(b) **Children.** bullet and table, placed after the Signals table, is the one home of what the supervisor reports for each child's exit. Rows: the launched runtime process, a sweep worker, the cleanup worker, each cleanup command, and processes reparented to the namespace init. Columns: status 0, non-zero, signalled, status not reaching its waiting step, exit racing a request. The table names the error kinds rather than codes, because the SPEC-7 card leaves the codes to the two binaries (CODE-2 maps them: `cleanup_timeout`, `cleanup_failed`, and the sweep and bound codes). Rule: one reaper (`wait4(-1, WNOHANG)` on SIGCHLD) hands each worker's status to its waiting step, and a lost status is a failure, never success. The Signals SIGCHLD row, the scrub table's `cleanupCommands` Error cell, the edge-case table, CODE-2 (`end`, `sweep`, `cleanup`, `exited`, and a new reaping bullet), and TEST-1 cite it — BECAUSE the cleanup failure conditions were listed in the scrub table cell, CODE-2, and the edge table and missed a signalled child and a worker status taken by the generic reaper (`[spec.23.fix-design-G1.1]` OPEN) — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the scrub table cell as the home (it has no row for the runtime process or for orphans, and no column for a lost status).
FACT: The cleanup worker stops at the first failed command, as `workspace.RunSetup` does today (it returns on the first command error). EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/setup.go `RunSetup`.
DECISION: A runtime process whose exit is reaped before `launched` is written is reported by `launched` and then `exited`, so the launch row's "exits before it holds the session" cell applies; an exit reaped while an `end` is in progress is part of the end. Both rules sit in the runtime row of the Children table.
DECISION: **Not-live latch.** SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** is rewritten as the one statement of the adapter's not-live state: every cause, that it refuses every session start (one that would use a live process included) and every `launch`, that it never clears, that `ReportPodScrub` reports it, that a cause before READY exits the adapter, and that a service-mode pod exits on any cause. **Launch.** (which let a start use a live process on a not-live pod), the scrub table's launch row (which guarded only on rows above it), the scrub table's *not live* and *link failure* definitions, **SDK-warm.**'s `DemoteSDK` sentence, SPEC-4 item 8's gate outcome, the edge-case rows, and CODE-3's state table now cite it — BECAUSE three statements disagreed and the deleted §4.7.10 sentence "refuses the next session's start" had nothing in its place (`[spec.23.review-reliability.1]`, `[spec.23.review-security.1]` WATCHOUT) — ALTERNATIVES: a separate latch table (rejected: a bullet with a cause list is enough and keeps one home).
DECISION: The hold timeout's close of the runtime connection, and a launched process that does not connect on `CH-MSGSOCK` within its starting session's deadline, are causes on every pod — BECAUSE §10.1 **Hold state timeout:** says that pod serves no later session, which SPEC-2(e) now points at **Liveness.**, and the old **Liveness.** listed a closed connection only on a `keep` pod, so a `restart` pod after a hold timeout had no stated refusal; the never-connected process is the `connected && !ended` case the tree's `SocketRuntimeProcess.ServesNextSession` already reports false — ALTERNATIVES: leaving both `keep`-only (fails open on a concurrent `restart` pod).
CORRECTS [spec.23.fix-G1.1 DECISION "the SPEC-5 scrub table `cleanupCommands` row Error cell stays the one home of the cleanup failure conditions"]: superseded; SPEC-2 **Children.** is the home, and the cell says "for each error code `cleaned` carries, which **Children.** states".
CORRECTS [spec.22.fix-G2.1 DECISION applying delegated decision (A)]: superseded; the fault signals have no handler and are blocked.
CORRECTS [redesign3.2026-10-22 DECISION "Sender identification stays in the self-test and the `restart` gating set"]: superseded by the owner's retirement.
CORRECTS [rebaseline.2026-10-22 DECISION "The property self-test gates on the non-dumpable mark, sender identification, and `kill(-1)` reach"]: sender identification no longer gates or is tested.
CORRECTS [f8.human-decisions OPEN on the "sender-identification retirement" directive]: the owner decision is real (2026-10-22); the open decision is removed.
WATCHOUT: `problem-statement.md` still records the plan's "acts on a stop only when `si_pid == 0`"; it is the problem as written and was not edited.

### [spec.24.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The `exited` step's single home is §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.**, staged in SPEC-2(d) as one inserted sentence after the release sentence; SPEC-2(b) **Crash.** now points to "the `exited` step of the §4.7.1 registry critical section" — BECAUSE the tree paragraph ends "This paragraph is the only statement of that atomicity." (spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.**), so a lock member stated only in §4.7.10 falsified it — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the "only statement" sentence (splits the invariant); leaving **Crash.** unchanged (two homes); putting the failure of each held session inside the §4.7.1 step (restates **Crash.**'s outcome).
WATCHOUT: Any new step the adapter performs under the slot-registry lock must be added to §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.**, whose closing sentence claims to be the only statement of that atomicity. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.**

### [spec.24.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the `exited` step of the registry lock gets its home in the tree paragraph §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.** (one sentence that SPEC-2(d) inserts after the "On every release" sentence), and SPEC-2(b) **Crash.** points to it — BECAUSE that paragraph says it is "the only statement of that atomicity" and the crash-versus-release race depends on this step — ALTERNATIVES: leaving the sentence in **Crash.** and deleting "only statement" from §4.7.1 (rejected: this splits one invariant across two homes); restating the step in both places (rejected: two statements of one rule).
WATCHOUT: the inserted §4.7.1 sentence names only the DECISION of which sessions the exited process holds, judged against the registry. It must not restate the **Crash.** outcome (`runtime_crash`, Liveness). The definition of "holds a session" (from the rule-8 record until deregistration) stays in **Crash.** — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:739; spec-changes.md SPEC-2(b) **Crash.** bullet.
FACT: in this proposal directory, `*spec-changes.md` matches both spec-changes.md and non-spec-changes.md, so a glob-based sed prints the wrong file's lines. Name the file in full.

### [spec.24.review-reliability.1]
FACT: spec §4.7.1 **The registry critical section.** enumerates every step performed under the registry lock and ends "This paragraph is the only statement of that atomicity." SPEC-2 **Crash.** adds a new atomic step (handling `exited` under the lock) outside that paragraph, and no staged edit touches §4.7.1's list. The release-row race resolution depends on that step. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:739; spec-changes.md:302-307
FACT: Deregistration before the `session_end` write is implied by §4.7.1 rules 12/14 ("removes that entry, performs both teardowns") plus the `Shutdown` row (teardown writes `session_end`); the release row's citation holds. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:688, rules 12 and 14
FACT: Reliability sweep of the supervised recovery paths found no other gap: supervisor death kills the namespace and surfaces as a link failure plus CH-MSGSOCK EOF (existing CH-ATTACH `runtime_crash` path); every request is bounded or backstopped by the gateway missing-report timeout; `exited` of a stale generation cannot arrive after `ended` because the stream is ordered and launch waits for an attested end.

### [spec.24.review-security.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings under the security lens for round 24 — BECAUSE every bound checked has a trusted authoritative source: the pod lifetime comes from the WPC-set immutable label `lenny.dev/runtime-process` (gateway) and the WPC-rendered adapter flag (adapter); an absent or unknown label reads as `keep`, which the per-pod acknowledgment refusal fails closed; the tenant pin already applies on every pool; and nothing new is Redis-backed — ALTERNATIVES: filing a missing-member rule for `ready.properties` (rejected: the property names are paired between the two platform binaries and pinned by the tier-3 contract test, so this is hardening and not a bypass); filing the keep-pod clause "a cleanup it disrupts fails the scrub" (rejected: owner-accepted residue, `[spec.23.follow-up-fix.1]`).

The note is not exhaustive (it already omits tenant-id), and the normative list is §17.2 item 5, which SPEC-5 does edit. So this is not a missed edit site. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:237; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:46.
FACT: The runtime container's writable mounts are workspace, tmp, sessions, artifacts, and dshm. The credential and shared volumes are read-only in it. This matches the SPEC-2 **Sweep.** list. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:37-47.

### [spec.26.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The rule-8 record of a session is taken after `session_started` inside the open sequence, so a process launched at step 7 holds no session until step 10; the launch row's "before the process holds the session" window and the Liveness cause "after its launch and before it holds its first session" both cover the launch-to-record gap. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:683; spec-changes.md:330-332, :577
FACT: A start that fails on a not-live pod leads to the pod's drain without a recycle boundary (§5.2 Runtime not live: "the start fails. On a pool with maxConcurrentSessions: 1 the gateway drains the pod"), so a cause set on an idle pod has a reclaimer. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:527
FACT: CH-SUPERVISE requests sent by the whole-pod scrub (`end`, `sweep`) have no adapter-side bound because the scrub has none; the gateway missing-report timeout retires the pod, and pod deletion reclaims the stuck adapter, so a hung supervisor during a sweep stalls nothing past that timeout. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:564, :759-763; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:493
DECISION: no reliability finding in round 26 — BECAUSE the only change (the §4.7.1 registry-critical-section insert for `exited`) puts the crash decision under the registry lock, which closes the exit-versus-deregistration race the release row describes; every other path traced (link failure, warm-up failure, hold timeout, cleanup-worker stop on a keep pod, pod termination) ends in retirement with a reclaimer — ALTERNATIVES: filing the supervisor dial window versus late adapter listener bind (pod is replaced, only slower recovery; settled in review-log lines 126 and 215).

DECISION: The §5.2 **Runtime not live:** home is re-keyed from "its runtime process" to "its runtime" as SPEC-5(i), mirrored in DOC-1's execution-modes.md bullet — BECAUSE on a `restart` pod no runtime process exists when `ReportPodScrub` is sampled, so the process-keyed home retired every scrubbed `restart` pod; the SPEC-2(d) row and **Liveness.** already say "runtime" — ALTERNATIVES: rewording **Liveness.** again (round 28 did; the divergent text was the unedited home); staging under SPEC-2(d) (SPEC-5 owns spec/05 §5.2).

DECISION: The reclaimer for a pod whose adapter exited while the runtime container still runs (frozen or hung supervisor, before READY or on a service-mode pod) is a §6.2 **Terminated adapter container.** paragraph staged as SPEC-2(f), with CODE-4 `observePod` returning `lifecycle.PodFailed` on a terminated `adapter` container status and TEST-1 tier-1 and tier-8 cases; the **Liveness.** bullet now points to §6.2 for the pod's outcome and D-SERVICE's "the failed pod is replaced" is deleted — BECAUSE restartPolicy Never keeps the Pod `Running` while the runtime container runs, and `Decide` leaves a Running not-Ready pod alone in warming and idle — ALTERNATIVES: exec probe (new surface), shareProcessNamespace (breaks the PID-init design), gateway drain-request (gateway holds no connection to warming or idle pods), init-immunity gate (D-PROPERTIES settled), edge parentheticals (two homes).
FACT: `observePod` keys only on Pod phase and Ready; `Decide` maps PodNotReady to no action in warming and idle, and PodFailed to warming→failed and idle→draining — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go (observePod), pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go (Decide); pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go (RestartPolicyNever)
WATCHOUT: Do not widen **Terminated adapter container.** to claimed phases (the claim and §6.2 **Pod crash during an active session.** own them) or key it on Pod phase Failed or Ready false (Ready false is also true while warming).

### [spec.30.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the §5.2 **Runtime not live:** item (spec/05) is re-keyed from "its runtime process" to "its runtime" as a new SPEC-5 sub-item — BECAUSE the item is the one home of the retire predicate (tier-11 TestKeptRuntimeRetireConditionHasOneSpecHome), and a `restart` pod has no runtime process when ReportPodScrub is sampled — ALTERNATIVES: editing the SPEC-2 **Liveness.** bullet again (round 28 already rewrote it; the home is §5.2, not the bullet); putting the edit in SPEC-2(d) (SPEC-2 does not touch spec/05, while SPEC-5 owns §5.2 and checklist S6 defers to the deliverable index).
FACT: the tier-11 one-home check exempts the spec/05 line carrying `**Runtime not live:**` and requires only `runtime_not_live` and `or omits that fact` on it, so "cannot serve the next session" may stay on that line — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/runtime_process_lifetime_doc_reconciliation_test.go:228-249
DECISION: the frozen or hung supervisor on a warming pod or an idle service-mode pod is reclaimed by one new rule: the WarmPoolController observes a pod whose `adapter` container has terminated as failed (new §6.2 **Terminated adapter container.** paragraph, SPEC-2(f); `observePod` in CODE-4) — BECAUSE restartPolicy Never keeps the Pod Running while the runtime container runs, observePod keys only on Pod phase, and Decide leaves a Running not-Ready pod alone in warming and idle; draining then deletes the Pod, and the kubelet's SIGKILL from outside the namespace ends a stopped supervisor — ALTERNATIVES: an exec liveness probe on the runtime container (new probe surface inside the agent's container); shareProcessNamespace so the adapter can kill the supervisor (breaks the namespace-init design); a gateway-stamped drain-request (the gateway holds no connection to a warming or idle pod); deleting "the pod is replaced" with nothing in its place (leaves the stuck pod).
FACT: the gap predates this proposal: any adapter non-zero exit before READY today leaves the unsupervised runtime container running and the Pod Running, not Ready, forever; the new rule closes that too — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:252-271 (observePod), pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:76-94
WATCHOUT: a claimed pod is not covered by the new paragraph and should not be: the claimed phases are projected from the claim (gateway-owned), and §6.2 **Pod crash during an active session.** already states that outcome. Do not widen the paragraph to claimed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:103-107
WATCHOUT: the embedded pod has no `adapter` container (its single container is `runtime`), so the rule is a no-op there by construction — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/embedded.go:59
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: S2 says SPEC-2 "Lands the §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** paragraph and its citing sites"; after SPEC-2(f) it also lands the spec/06 §6.2 **Terminated adapter container.** paragraph, which is a cited site rather than a citing one. S18's "CODE-4 with its tier-1 and tier-2 tests" stays true (the observePod case is tier 1).
OPEN: docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:86, docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md:403, and schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:528 (runtime_live comment, including the connection-open liveness rule SPEC-2(d) removed) still describe the ReportPodScrub liveness fact as being about the runtime process. That is a separate finding against SPEC-2(d) and DOC-1/D-SURFACE ("No proto file changes"), not caused by this round.

FACT: The label lifetime is safe under informer lag in both directions. A stale cache that stamps `keep` after a keep→restart edit yields a used pod the acquisition refuses and drains. Reading an absent label as `keep` (Postgres fallback mirror) refuses rather than admits. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5(b) and (g)
FACT: Pod termination is consistent with kubelet ordering. Only the adapter container carries the preStop hook (sidecar.go:95), so the runtime container gets its stop signal at once, and the Signals table's "None" action for that signal keeps the runtime alive through the adapter's preStop checkpoint. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:95, spec/04_system-components.md:509

### [spec.30.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The WarmPoolController's warm-path planner leaves a Running pod whose Ready condition is false untouched in `warming` and in `idle` ("PodPending, PodNotReady — the pod is still warming" / "leave it warm"); only Pod phase Failed or Succeeded, or absence, moves it. A pod with one terminated container and one running container stays phase Running under `restartPolicy: Never`. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:76-95, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:252-270, pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:37
FACT: Every recovery the staging calls "the adapter exits non-zero and the pod is replaced" depends on the supervisor's exit-on-close: that exit ends the runtime container so the Pod reaches phase Failed. A supervisor that is stopped (SIGSTOP reaches it where PID-namespace init is not immune, which the Signals table allows) cannot exit, so the runtime container keeps running, the pod stays Running and NotReady, and nothing reclaims it. The runtime container has no probe. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:322-324, :362, :382-383; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:96-107
WATCHOUT: Fix the frozen-supervisor reclaim gap with a reclaimer, not by making init immunity a gate: D-PROPERTIES (immunity is reported only) is an owner-settled decision. EVIDENCE: review-log Standing context, "Owner decisions of 2026-10-22 on the supervisor's security review"

### [redesign5.2026-10-22]
DECISION: Owner decision of 2026-10-22: the WarmPoolController defect (`observePod` keys only on Pod phase and Ready, so a terminated `adapter` container on a pod that `restartPolicy: Never` keeps `Running` is invisible; `Decide` has no `failed` arm, so `failed ──→ draining` never runs) is fixed by a separate precursor direct fix on `fix/warmpool-reclaim-terminated-adapter` that lands before 0087. SPEC-2(f) **Terminated adapter container.**, the CODE-4 `observePod`/`Decide` bullet and targets, and the TEST-1 tier-1 `observePod`/`Decide`/`DecideSDKWarm` cases are removed. The precondition is recorded in summary D-PREREQS, **Watch out for.**, and **Defects in the shipped tree**, and in the checklist preamble — BECAUSE the defect predates 0087 and affects every pod whose adapter exits before READY — ALTERNATIVES: keep the one-paragraph controller patch in SPEC-2 (rejected by the owner).
DECISION: One table, the reclaim table, under SPEC-2(b) §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.** **Liveness.**, derived from `observePod` and `podReady` (`pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go`), `Decide` and `DecideSDKWarm` (`pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go`), the §6.2 fenced edges, `Binder.Release` (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go`), the §4.6.3 "ends the occupancy without reuse" rule, the `ReportPodScrub` row, and §5.2 **Runtime not live:** and **Slot retry policy**. Rows: `warming`; `sdk_connecting` on the warm-fill edge; `idle`; `reserved`; `claimed` with a `bound` claim, sequential and concurrent; `claimed` with a `recycling` claim while the whole-pod scrub runs; `sdk_connecting` on the recycle re-warm edge; and termination (`draining`, or any phase once the pod is being deleted). Columns: service-mode, session `keep`, `restart`, `preConnect`. Each cell names its causes and one channel — BECAUSE the reclaim path was spread across six sites and each sweep found one missing or conflicting cell — ALTERNATIVES: home in §6.2 (rejected: the cells are the adapter's causes, which **Liveness.** owns, so a §6.2 home would split the latch across two sections; §6.2 keeps the edges, which the table cites); the owner's seven rows unsplit (rejected: `sdk_connecting` has two legs on opposite sides of READY with different channels, and the `recycling` scrub is the only phase in which **Report** carries a cause, so each got its own row).
DECISION: Channels: **Exit** (the adapter exits non-zero; the WarmPoolController, per the precursor, observes a terminated `adapter` container on a pod not being deleted as failed: `warming ──→ failed`, `sdk_connecting ──→ failed`, then `failed ──→ draining`, or `idle ──→ draining`); **Start** (the next start fails, which claims the pod, and **Claim** retires it); **Claim** (the release that ends the occupancy retires the pod when it ends it without reuse, which a refused start's release, a failed or crashed session's release on a sequential pod, and every release on a non-recycling pool do; otherwise **Report**; on a concurrent pod failed or refused starts also count toward the whole-pod replacement trigger); **Report** (`ReportPodScrub` at the end of the whole-pod scrub); **None** (the pod is being deleted). A cell names the channel that retires the pod before it serves another session; an earlier path (a failed session, the uptime limit, an SDK-warm watchdog) changes nothing a cell states — BECAUSE the owner's four channel names overlap on a sequential pod (a refused start and the boundary report both act through the claim disposition), so each is defined by where it hands off — ALTERNATIVES: a cell per cause (rejected: the owner asked for one channel per cell).
FACT: A refused start's release ends the occupancy without reuse, because no gateway replica published the session's binding (§4.6.3 SandboxClaim ownership row: "when no gateway replica published the session's binding to the pod ... or, on a pool with `maxConcurrentSessions: 1`, when the session failed or crashed"). The gateway's candidate scan does not read Pod readiness, so an `idle` pod whose runtime container died is still claimed and its start fails. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.3; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle (no Pod Ready read outside the pool warming-up condition)
DECISION: The §6.2 SIGTERM edge `sdk_connecting ──→ terminated` stays unedited and the table's **None** channel cites it: on a sidecar pod `ExitOnSignal` runs `ShutdownDemoteSDK`, which reaches `EndProcess` (`end`), so the runtime ends by an `end` and sets no cause, and **Exit** excludes a pod that is being deleted, which keeps the edge true — BECAUSE a precursor that reported the exited adapter of a deleted pod as failed would take `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` instead — ALTERNATIVES: edit the edge for the sidecar model (rejected: its text already holds).
DECISION: The fenced `idle ──→ draining` cause list and the warm-fill `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` cause list each gain "or the adapter exits — see §4.7.10 Runtime supervisor" (SPEC-2(f)); `warming ──→ failed (warm-fill failure)` and `failed ──→ draining` already cover **Exit** — BECAUSE the table's **Exit** cell for `idle` and for the warm-fill `sdk_connecting` takes those edges and the fence did not list the cause — ALTERNATIVES: a §6.2 paragraph (rejected: the owner removed it); leaving the fence (rejected: the table and the fence would disagree).
DECISION: *hang* and a `CH-MSGSOCK` end while the process holds a session are causes on every pod, and *kept-process exit* stays `keep`-only — BECAUSE on a concurrent `restart` pod later sessions join a live process (**Launch.**), so a `keep`-only hang cause let a hung process take new sessions until occupancy reached zero (`[spec.30.review-fresh.1]` OPEN); a connection end or exit after the release's deregistration holds no session, so the scrub table's release row keeps its "no crash, and next" outcome — ALTERNATIVES: an accepted bound under D-RESTARTGATE (rejected: simple and fail-closed wins, and the gate is interim); making every exit a cause on every pod (rejected: it would retire a `restart` pod whose process exits just after its last release, which the release row reuses).
DECISION: D-HUNG rewritten: a heartbeat escalation sets the not-live state on every pod, which refuses every start (one that would use the hung process included) and makes the next report retire the pod — BECAUSE the old text said only "for the next boundary report", understating the latch's refusal, and relied on a `restart` pod's boundary `end`, true only on a sequential pod — ALTERNATIVES: none considered.
DECISION: Retargeted to the table: the **Liveness.** prose (dropped "§6.2 states the pod's outcome" and "so the pod retires under Runtime not live whatever onScrubFailure states", now in the **Report** channel); §5.2 **Runtime not live:** (SPEC-5(i) appends one citation sentence on the same physical line); SPEC-7(d) **Degradation.** (the pre-READY exit retires the pod as the table states, replacing "the pod is never marked idle"); SPEC-7(f) §28.8 row (columns 2, 3, and 5); the edge-case table (every row that cited **Liveness.** retargeted, and rows added for a latched `idle` or `reserved` pod, and a pod deleted with the state set); D-HUNG, D-NOTLIVE, D-SERVICE, D-SPEC-HOMES; CODE-3 (state section, crash, hang, connection-end); TEST-1 (a tier-1 table-driven case per reachable cell; tier-8 **Exit** and **Start** cases).
DECISION: D-SURFACE and the non-spec "No change to `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto`" sentence are corrected: no message, field, or RPC changes, and CODE-3 rewrites three doc comments that SPEC-2 makes false (`Shutdown` "stays alive for the pod's life", `ReportPodScrubRequest` "reported live whether or not it answers heartbeats", `RunSetupRequest` "initializes once per pod"), regenerates `pkg/proto/adapter/v1` (protoc-gen-go copies leading comments), and rewrites the `Shutdown` comment in `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go`; DOC-1 gains the matching `adapter-contract.md` and `lifecycle.md` sites — BECAUSE `[spec.30.fix-G1.1]` OPEN and `[spec.30.fix-design-G1.1]` OPEN left the mirrors unstaged — ALTERNATIVES: a separate proto proposal (rejected: comment-only, no wire change).
CORRECTS [spec.30.fix-G1.1 DECISION "The reclaimer ... is a §6.2 **Terminated adapter container.** paragraph staged as SPEC-2(f), with CODE-4 `observePod` ..."]: withdrawn; the precursor fix carries the controller change, and SPEC-2(f) now edits only the two fenced cause lists.
CORRECTS [spec.30.follow-up-fix.1 DECISION adding `Decide` `failed → draining` to CODE-4]: withdrawn; that is the precursor's reclaimer.
CORRECTS [rebaseline.2026-10-22 DECISION "F-5.2.46 is closed by marking the runtime not live on a heartbeat escalation on a `keep` pod"]: the escalation is a cause on every pod.
WATCHOUT: The new **Liveness.** text and table are in spec/04 and must not carry "cannot serve the next session" or "runtime_not_live" (tier-11 TestKeptRuntimeRetireConditionHasOneSpecHome); they say "not able to serve" and name the reason by its label "Runtime not live". The SPEC-5(i) appended sentence sits on the §5.2 home line, where the phrase is allowed. EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/runtime_process_lifetime_doc_reconciliation_test.go
UNVERIFIED: The precursor branch `fix/warmpool-reclaim-terminated-adapter` had no changes in its worktree when this redesign ran, so its exact behavior (in particular the exclusion of a pod being deleted, and whether it edits spec text) is assumed from the owner's description. Reconcile SPEC-2(f) and the **Exit** and **None** channel definitions against it when it lands.

### [redesign5.idle-exit.2026-10-22]
DECISION: Owner decision of 2026-10-22, taken under delegation: a cause of the not-live state that arises while the adapter's registry holds no session entry and no whole-pod scrub runs (the `Sandbox` phases `idle` and `reserved` and the recycle re-warm edge of `sdk_connecting`, on every kind of sidecar pod) exits the adapter non-zero at once, so those **Liveness.** table cells retire through **Exit** rather than **Start** — BECAUSE waiting for a session start left unbounded the time a latched pod that serves no session keeps its place in the pool. **Start** is withdrawn, because no cell reaches it; a refused or failed start remains part of **Claim**. A cause during a registered session or during the whole-pod scrub does not exit the adapter, so **Claim** and **Report** are unchanged. Applied to SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** prose, the **Exit** and **Claim** channel definitions, the `idle`, `reserved`, and re-warm `sdk_connecting` rows, the edge-case row, SPEC-2(f) (a third fenced edit adds the adapter's exit to the re-warm `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` edge), CODE-3 (idle-exit bullet), TEST-1 tiers 1 and 8, and summary D-NOTLIVE — ALTERNATIVES: keep **Start** for `reserved` (rejected: a rebind still fails its start under **Claim**, and hold expiry otherwise returns the pod to `idle`, where it would wait again).
FACT: The bound for a `reserved` pod is the hold, not the orphan timeout. The precursor's planner takes no action on `reserved` (`lifecycle.Decide` default arm), and the occupancy projection keeps `reserved` while the claim is `reserved`; the hold ends at `holdExpiresAt` (`gateway.claimHoldTTLSeconds`, default 10 s) by the gateway's DELETE, or at `holdExpiresAt` plus `ReservedHoldGrace` by the collector's reserved predicate, and the claim's absence projects `reserved → idle`, after which `Decide` takes `idle → draining` on `PodFailed`. A rebind before then fails its start, and the release ends the occupancy without reuse (§4.6.3, binding not published). EVIDENCE: precursor worktree pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go `Decide` default arm; pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go `ProjectOccupancyPhase` (no claim on reserved → idle); pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go `classify` (`claimstate.Reserved`); spec/04 §4.6.1 **Orphaned `SandboxClaim` detection:** item 2.
FACT: On the re-warm edge `DecideSDKWarm` takes `sdk_connecting → failed` on `PodFailed` on both edges, `HoldFailedReclaim` then holds `failed → draining` while the `recycling` claim exists, and the projection leaves a `failed` Sandbox alone unless the claim is `released` or `failed`. The gateway's `pollRewarm` stops at its budget without retiring, so the claim ends only through the collector's live-state predicate, `claimOrphanTimeout` (default 15 minutes) after the claim's last binding-state transition. EVIDENCE: precursor pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go `DecideSDKWarm`, `HoldFailedReclaim`; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `Reconcile`; pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go; draft tree pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go `pollRewarm`; pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go `drainSandbox` (no-op on a terminal Sandbox) then claim DELETE.
FACT: The precursor reduces a terminated `adapter` or `runtime` container to `PodFailed` whatever the Pod phase and does not consult the Pod's deletion timestamp; only a Sandbox with a deletion timestamp goes to the finalizer path, which predates the precursor. EVIDENCE: precursor pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `observePod`, `agentContainerTerminated`; BUILD-GAPS F-6.2.31 resolution.
DECISION: The **Exit** definition keeps its exclusion of a Pod that is being deleted, so the spec stays consistent with the §6.2 SIGTERM edge `sdk_connecting ──→ terminated`, and the precursor's divergence (a deleted `sdk_connecting` pod reaches `terminated` through `failed ──→ draining`) is recorded with the existing `PodAbsent` defect in the summary's defects section rather than asserted away — BECAUSE the earlier text claimed the precursor implements the exclusion, which its code does not; no pod is stranded either way. The **Exit** definition also names the `runtime` container, which the precursor observes, and states that `failed ──→ draining` waits for the pod's claim to end.
DECISION: The CODE-3 `Shutdown` proto doc comment says a `restart` pod's process ends in the next whole-pod scrub or when the pod retires — BECAUSE a release that ends the occupancy without reuse retires the pod with no scrub.
DECISION: The summary's precursor risk bullet now says the fix lands before S1 — BECAUSE the checklist precondition says before S1 and the bullet said before S2.
CORRECTS [redesign5.2026-10-22 UNVERIFIED "The precursor branch ... had no changes in its worktree"]: resolved against commits c2acb6a41 and 14157668c; see the FACT lines above. The deletion exclusion is not implemented.
CORRECTS [redesign5.2026-10-22 DECISION "Channels: **Exit** ... **Start** ..."]: **Start** withdrawn; **Exit** covers `idle`, `reserved` (once the hold ends), and the re-warm edge (once the claim ends).
CORRECTS [redesign5.2026-10-22 DECISION "The §6.2 SIGTERM edge ... **Exit** excludes a pod that is being deleted, which keeps the edge true"]: the spec text keeps the exclusion, but the precursor does not honor it; recorded as a shipped-tree defect.

### [precursor-c29693b62.2026-10-22]
FACT: Precursor commit `c29693b62` makes `observePod` return `PodTerminating` for a Pod with a deletion timestamp before any other check. `Decide` and `DecideSDKWarm` never route `PodTerminating` through `failed`: `sdk_connecting ──→ terminated` on the warm-fill and the recycle re-warm edge, `idle ──→ draining` with `draining` waiting for the pod to go without re-issuing the delete, and `warming` holding (no promotion, no `failed`, no pod create) until the pod is gone, after which the warming arm creates a fresh pod. `failed`, `claimed`, `reserved`, and `terminated` are unchanged, and the readiness gate is never flipped on a terminating pod. `DecideSDKWarm` still takes `sdk_connecting ──→ failed` on `PodAbsent`. EVIDENCE: precursor worktree commit c29693b62, pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go `observePod`, `syncReadinessGate`; pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go `Decide`, `DecideSDKWarm`, `holdTerminatingWarming`; BUILD-GAPS F-6.2.31 "Resolution, deleted pods".
DECISION: D-PREREQS, the summary's precursor risk bullet, the checklist precondition, the CODE-4 no-change bullet, and the SPEC-2(f) closing sentence state that the precursor observes a deleted pod as terminating and follows the §6.2 SIGTERM edge, with the warming hold. The SPEC-2(b) **Exit** and **None** channel text already excludes a pod being deleted and needs no edit. The defects-section entry is narrowed to the `PodAbsent` case, which `c29693b62` leaves in place — BECAUSE the precursor now honors the exclusion.
CORRECTS [redesign5.idle-exit.2026-10-22 FACT "The precursor reduces a terminated `adapter` or `runtime` container to `PodFailed` whatever the Pod phase and does not consult the Pod's deletion timestamp"]: superseded by `c29693b62`, which checks the deletion timestamp first.
CORRECTS [redesign5.idle-exit.2026-10-22 DECISION "The **Exit** definition keeps its exclusion of a Pod that is being deleted ... the precursor's divergence ... is recorded with the existing `PodAbsent` defect"]: the precursor honors the exclusion after `c29693b62`; only the `PodAbsent` divergence stays in the defects section.
CORRECTS [redesign5.idle-exit.2026-10-22 CORRECTS "The deletion exclusion is not implemented" and "the precursor does not honor it; recorded as a shipped-tree defect"]: the deletion exclusion is implemented in `c29693b62`, and the shipped-tree defect entry for it is removed.

### [spec.31.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The exit-at-once predicate in SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** is keyed on session history: a cause exits the adapter at once when it arises after READY or after a whole-pod scrub ends and before the next session entry is registered. BECAUSE an empty registry also holds between the last entry's deregistration and the scrub's start, and a kept-process exit on `session_end` falls in that gap, where the table says **Report**. ALTERNATIVES: a "scrub due" flag set by the recycle Shutdown was rejected because on a concurrent pod `Binder.ReleaseSlot` sends a plain Shutdown first and `ShutdownRecycle` only after `releaseSlotRetrying`, and because a release without reuse sends no recycle Shutdown. Keying on the `Sandbox` phase was rejected because the adapter cannot read it.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce "registry holds no session entry" as the exit predicate anywhere. The registry is empty in the deregistration-to-scrub gap of every recycle (session mode: `answerShutdown` deregisters and tears down before `startPodScrub`; concurrent: two Shutdowns in `Binder.ReleaseSlot`). EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go `answerShutdown`; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go `ReleaseSlot`
FACT: `startPodScrub` (pkg/adapter/podscrub.go) runs the scrub in a goroutine that ends through `defer s.signalScrubDone()`. That is where CODE-3's registered-since-scrub field clears, and `createSlotStateLocked` (pkg/adapter/slot.go) is where it is set. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/podscrub.go `startPodScrub`
DECISION: The preConnect `idle` cell gains *session exit*. BECAUSE a warm-fill `idle` pod holds a warm-up process that has never held a session, and the *session exit* definition covers that process's exit. ALTERNATIVES: the same addition to preConnect `reserved` was rejected because a `reserved` pod is reached only through a recycle, so its kept process has already held a session.
DECISION: The edge-case row for an idle, reserved, or re-warm cause is cut to a pointer at the **Liveness.** table. BECAUSE the **Exit** channel definition is the home of the hold and claimOrphanTimeout bounds.
DEFERRED [0087_new_under-the-default-sidecar-deployment-model-spec-4-7.implementation-checklist.md]: no step names the changed predicate or the edge-case row (grepped "idle exit", "scrub runs", and "no session"), so nothing is owed. The CODE-3 step that lands the Idle-exit bullet now also lands the registered-since-scrub field, and this needs to be confirmed when the checklist is reconciled.

### [spec.31.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the Liveness exit-at-once predicate keys on "after READY or the end of a whole-pod scrub, and before the next session entry is registered", replacing "registry holds no session entry and no whole-pod scrub runs" — BECAUSE the registry is empty between the last entry's deregistration and the scrub's start, and a kept process exiting on its session_end there must reach Report — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "no scrub is due or runs (a recycle Shutdown makes one due)" was rejected because on a concurrent pod `Binder.ReleaseSlot` sends an unconditional Shutdown first and the recycle Shutdown only after the Redis slot release and the claim's recycling patch, so no adapter-visible "due" state exists in that gap; a release without reuse leaves the same gap with no recycle Shutdown at all.
FACT: concurrent-pod last-slot release is two adapter Shutdowns: unconditional (deregisters), then `ShutdownRecycle` after `releaseSlotRetrying` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go `ReleaseSlot`; session-mode recycle Release sends one Shutdown and `answerShutdown` starts the scrub after teardown — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go `answerShutdown`.
FACT: session entries are created only in `createSlotStateLocked` (pkg/adapter/slot.go); once one exists the pod never returns to idle without a scrub end, because every release either ends the occupancy without reuse (Claim) or recycles (§4.6.3 field-ownership row for SandboxClaim).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce an empty-registry test for the exit; the discriminating TEST-1 case is a kept-process exit between the last deregistration and the scrub's start, on a sequential and on a concurrent pod.
DECISION: preConnect `reserved` cell does not gain session exit — BECAUSE `reserved` is reached only through a recycle, and the re-warm edge launches nothing, so the process has held a session (kept-process exit only).

### [spec.31.follow-up-fix.1]
CORRECTS [previous fixer's idle-exit field]: the registered-since-scrub field cleared at the end of `startPodScrub`'s goroutine left a window, from `sampleRuntimeLiveness` until `ReportPodScrub` returns, in which a cause was latched with no exit and a `runtime_live: true` report — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/podscrub.go `startPodScrub` (sample, then the RPC on `context.Background()`, then goroutine end).
DECISION: the clear of that field exits the adapter non-zero when `notLive` is set and the goroutine sent no `ReportPodScrub` carrying `runtime_live: false` (CODE-3 Idle exit); SPEC-2(b) **Liveness.** names the window's start as the scrub report's sample of the runtime as able to serve and lets the exit wait for that report to return — BECAUSE exiting at the sample would preempt the in-flight report, and exiting at the clear keeps "the exit never preempts that report" — ALTERNATIVES: exit at once after the sample (preempts the report, leaves a `claimed`/`recycling` pod on the **Exit** channel the table does not list); re-sample after the RPC and send a second report (no second-report contract exists).
WATCHOUT: a scrub whose goroutine sends no report (no `ScrubOps`, no reporter) also exits at the clear when `notLive` is set; that is intended, since the missing-report timeout retires the pod either way.
FACT: summary D-NOTLIVE's "until the whole-pod scrub ends" sentence and TEST-1's "before the scrub ends" case were the citers this change falsified; both now name the liveness sample. Remaining "after a whole-pod scrub" hits (summary D-NOTLIVE, CODE-3 proto comment) stay true.

### [spec.31.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed three findings: (1) the **Liveness.** exit-at-once predicate ("registry holds no session entry and no whole-pod scrub runs") is met between the occupancy-zero `Shutdown`'s deregistration and the start of its asynchronous scrub, where the table says **Report** (keep `claimed recycling`) — BECAUSE §4.7.1 rule 12/14 deregister before the teardown writes `session_end`, and the `Shutdown` row runs the scrub "asynchronously"; (2) the preConnect `idle` cell omits *session exit*, which is the cause for a warm-up process that has held no session; (3) edge-case row 47 restates the **Exit** channel's reserved and re-warm timing in full — ALTERNATIVES: SPEC-2(a) "lives ... to the next whole-pod scrub" versus CODE-3's "or when the pod retires" (judged below the bar); §29.2 step 23 / §7.1 "DemoteSDK also fails, the pod transitions to failed" versus the **Claim** channel (pre-existing text, not made wrong here).
FACT: Shutdown ordering: rules 12 and 14 remove the entry, then the teardowns run (flush usage, write `session_end`, end runtime use), and the recycle disposition's scrub runs asynchronously after that. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:688, :756-775 (rules 10-15)
FACT: A preConnect pod reaches `idle` with a warm-up process that has never held a session (§6.2 warm-fill `sdk_connecting ──→ idle`), so by the cause definitions its exit is *session exit*, not *kept-process exit*. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:88-89; spec-changes.md:340-350
WATCHOUT: Registry entries are created by `PrepareWorkspace`/`FinalizeWorkspace`/`RunSetup`/etc. (rule 4), so a freshly claimed pod has an empty registry before the gateway's first RPC; a cause there exits the adapter at once, but the failed first RPC still retires the pod through **Claim** (binding never published, §4.6.3). Not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:697, :619

### [spec.31.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens for round 31 — BECAUSE every redesign-5 retirement path traced through crash and restart ends in a bounded reclaim: `idle` (adapter exit, precursor PodFailed, `idle ──→ draining`), `reserved` (hold expiry or collector reserved predicate, then `idle ──→ draining`; a rebind fails its start and the unpublished-binding release ends the occupancy without reuse), re-warm edge (`failed` held until the `recycling` claim ends, at most `claimOrphanTimeout`), whole-pod scrub (Report, or the gateway missing-report timeout if the adapter is gone), and termination (None) — ALTERNATIVES: filing warm-fill churn when a gating property or supervisor dial fails deterministically on every pod (rejected: pre-existing warm-fill failure handling with `WarmPoolReplenishmentFailing`, and fail-closed); filing the prose "exit at once while the registry holds no session entry" versus the `claimed`/`bound` cell's **Claim** for a cause before the start registers (rejected: the adapter's exit makes the start fail, and the unpublished-binding release is exactly **Claim**, so the outcome agrees)
FACT: the missing-scrub-report grace is already operator-tunable in code (`MissingReportGracePeriod`, default 15 s, overridable) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go:24-31, :154, :211
FACT: the adapter container is listed before the runtime container, so the kubelet starts the adapter (and its `@lenny-supervise` listener) first; the supervisor's dial-retry window covers only the adapter's own startup — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:75-98
FACT: orphan predicate 1 (live `bound`/`recycling` claims) requires that no active session references the pod through `pod_assignment`; it is the backstop for a `recycling` claim whose adapter exited on the re-warm edge — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517
USEFUL [redesign5.idle-exit.2026-10-22 FACT lines on reserved and re-warm bounds]: they let the `reserved` and re-warm cells be checked without re-deriving the precursor's `Decide`/`HoldFailedReclaim` paths.
